\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\widehat{D}_{0}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}.

Hence, by the induction assumption, we conclude the statement.

∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Let us assume that
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
. The space
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)
is complete and

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{0}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)\cong C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}(X_{0},E_{0},\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}):(\widehat{D}_{0})^{-1}

is an isomorphism of Banach spaces and satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0})}\lesssim\left|\left|\widehat{D}_{0}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}}.

Proof.

We only need to show that the operator is closed. This follows from the fact that

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{0;H}:{\mathrm{dom}}_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}(\widehat{D}_{0;H};+)\rightarrow C^{k-1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}(X_{0},E_{0},\nabla^{\otimes_{0}})

is closed and
{\mathrm{dom}}_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}(\widehat{D}_{0;H})\subset{\mathrm{dom}}(D_{0;V})
where as
D_{0;V}
is bounded. Hence
\widehat{D}_{0}
is closed. The inverse is constructed using the right-inverse
R_{\Lambda,\mu}
, in (
4.2.2
),

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}(X_{0},E_{0},\nabla^{\otimes_{0}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{\Lambda,\mu_{\Lambda}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-R_{\Lambda,\mu_{\Lambda}}({\mathrm{cl}}_{0}(\Psi_{\Lambda,\mu_{\Lambda}})).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{D}_{0})^{-1}}

∎

In the theory of operator pencils (see
[
AN61
]
for the study of cylindrical operators) the space of logarithmic solutions is important in asymptotic expansions of kernel and cokernels of asymptotically conical or conically singular operators. These solutions can not exists in the present setup as seen in section
4.2
. Nevertheless, we will explicitly show that these solutions can not arise in the following line of arguments.

Definition 5.10
.

We define the space of
\beta
-logarithmic solutions

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)=\left\{\Phi=\left.\sum_{i=0}^{n}\log^{i}(r)\Phi_{i}\in\Gamma_{CF;\beta}(X_{0},E_{0};+)\right|\widehat{D}_{0}\Phi=0,\Phi_{i}\,\text{is }\beta\text{-homogeneous}\right\}.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\beta<0
. The space of
\beta
-logarithmic solutions of
\widehat{D}_{0}
can be identified with the space

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)={\mathrm{ker}}\left(B_{\beta;H}:\Gamma(S,\mathcal{E}_{\beta})\rightarrow\Gamma(S,\mathcal{E}_{-\beta})\right).

Proof.

The proof follows the ideas of Bera in
[
Ber23
]
. Let
\Phi=\sum_{i=0}^{l}\log^{i}(r)\Phi_{i}\in\mathcal{L}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)
. Then
B\Phi=0
is equivalent to

\displaystyle B\Phi_{l}=0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptB\Phi_{i}=-\frac{i+1}{r}\Phi_{i+1}.

The first equation reveals that
\Phi_{n}\sim r^{\beta}
. Hence, by

\displaystyle B\Phi_{n-1}=-\frac{n}{r}\Phi_{n}

reveals that
B_{H}\Phi_{l-1}=0
as the right hand side is homogeneous of degree
\beta-1
, but

\displaystyle B_{H}\Phi_{l-1}\sim r^{\beta}.

Hence,

\displaystyle(\partial_{r}+B_{V})\Phi_{l}=0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptB_{V}\Phi_{l-1}=-\frac{l}{r}\Phi_{l}.

Now taking the vertical
L^{2}
-product

\displaystyle 0\leq l\left<\Phi_{l},\Phi_{l}\right>_{L^{2}_{V}}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}\left<\Phi_{l},r(\partial_{r}+B_{V})\Phi_{l-1}\right>_{L^{2}_{r;Y}}r^{-1}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left<(\partial_{r}+B_{V})\Phi_{l},\Phi_{l-1}\right>_{L^{2}_{Cyl(Y)}}=0.

∎

5.4.
Uniform Elliptic Estimates for Conically Fibred Dirac Operators

As we have already discussed in section
3.3
the dilation action of
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
on the CF Dirac bundle, induces a family of Dirac bundle structures. In section
7
we will compare a family of ACF-Dirac operators to the family of CF-Dirac operators, rather than the fixed CF-Dirac operator
\widehat{D}_{0}
. This procedure is needed to obtain a uniform understanding of the adiabatic limit
t\to 0
.

The first lemma will clarify how the weighted Hölder norms, behave under the dilation action.

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
\Phi\in\Gamma^{k,\alpha}_{loc}(X_{0},E_{0})
. Then

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi|\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}=t^{-\beta-\delta(E_{0})}\left|\left|\Phi|\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}}

Proof.

This directly follows from the definition of the weighted Hölder norms.
∎

The following theorem states the uniformally of the family of operators
\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}
in
t
.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
\Phi\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)

(50)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi|\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}_{0})}\lesssim\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}}.

Proof.

This follows automatically from
4.1
and the definition of the weighted Schauder norms.
∎

6.
Elliptic Theory for Dirac Operators on Orbifolds as CSF Spaces

In the following we will analyse the Dirac operator on the orbifold
(X,g)
. As we have established in section
2
orbifolds are special edge spaces. In particular, if the orbifold only has type
(i)
singularities, normal cone bundles are CF spaces and hence a the local model for function spaces should include both the CF analysis and the „compact“ part of the orbifold. In contrast to the standard weighted function spaces for iterated edge spaces we will use more refined version by introducing function spaces on conically singular fibred spaces. We again establish improved Schauder estimates in CSF-Hölder norms and prove that a (left) semi-Fredholm property of an analytic realisation of the Dirac operator. In particular, the major difference to the existent literature we we introduce a family of Banach spaces and prove that this family is in a sense uniform.

6.0.1.
Unweighted Elliptic Theory for Dirac Operators on Orbifolds

Let
(X,g)
be a compact Riemannian orbifold and
(E,{\mathrm{cl}}_{g},\nabla^{h},h)
a Hermitian Dirac bundle.

Remark 39
.

As
(X,g)
is compact, there exists a good cover of
(X,g)
by action Lie groupoids, i.e. by Lie groupoids of the form

\displaystyle\Gamma\ltimes B_{\epsilon}(0)\rightrightarrows B_{\epsilon}(0).

\heartsuit

Furthermore, elliptic theory yields the following statement on compact orbifolds.

Proposition 6.1
.

Suppose
D\Phi=\Psi
is a weak solution. Then
\Phi\in W^{0,p}(X,E)
and
\Psi\in W^{l,p}(X,E)
implies that
\Phi\in W^{l+k,p}(X,E)
. Moreover,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{W^{l+k,p}}\lesssim\left|\left|D\Phi\right|\right|_{W^{l,p}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{W^{0,p}}.

If
\Phi\in C^{0}(X,E)
and
\Psi\in C^{k,\alpha}(X,E)
then
\Phi\in C^{k,\alpha}(X,E)
and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{l+k,\alpha}}\lesssim\left|\left|D\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}.

Using the Rellich-Kondrakov embeddings we immediately deduce the Fredholm property of the Sobolev and Hölder realisations of the Dirac operator
D
.

Theorem 6.1
.

The analytic realisations

(51)

\displaystyle D:

\displaystyle W^{k+1,p}(X,E)\rightarrow W^{k,p}(X,E)

(52)

\displaystyle D:

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}(X,E)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}(X,E)

are Fredholm. Moreover, their kernels coincide and do not depend on
k
,
p
or
\alpha

6.0.2.
Weighted Function Spaces on CSF Spaces

Let in the following
q:(Y,g_{Y})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
be a fibre bundle and a Riemannian submersion of compact Riemannian manifolds. Further, the Riemannian structure on
Y
induces an Ehresmann connection on the fibration, i.e.
TY\cong H_{Y}\oplus VY
.

Definition 6.1
.

A singular Riemannian manifold
(X,g)
is called conically singular fibred (CSF) of rate
\sigma
if there exists a compact subset
K_{R}
and a diffeomorphism
\rho:X\backslash K_{R}\rightarrow B_{R}(Y)
such that

\displaystyle g_{CSF}=g_{CSF;0}+g_{CSF;hot}\hskip 28.45274pt|g_{CSF;hot}|_{g_{CF}}=\mathcal{O}(r^{\sigma})

and

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF}})^{k}\left(\rho_{*}g_{CSF;0}-g_{CF}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{\sigma})

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF}}_{V})^{k}\left(\rho_{*}g_{CSF;0}-g_{CF}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{\sigma-k}).

By pulling pack the projection
p
as well as the connection
H_{CF}
be obtain a fibre bundle

\displaystyle\tilde{p}:X\backslash K_{R}\rightarrow S\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptT(X\backslash K_{R})\cong H_{CSF}\oplus V(X\backslash K_{R}).

Example 6
.

A Riemannian orbifold
(X,g)
whose singular strata are singular of type (I) are CSF spaces as

\displaystyle j:B_{R}(Y)\rightleftarrows{\mathrm{Tub}}_{R}(S):\rho.

Riemannian orbifolds of general type are quasi conically singular fibred (QCSF).

\clubsuit

Remark 40
.

Notice that all conically singular fibred spaces are (iterated) edge spaces.

\heartsuit

Definition 6.2
.

An CSF weight function is a smooth function coinciding with the distance function
w_{CSF}={\mathrm{dist}}_{g}(S)
in a tubular neighbourhood of
S
. Set

\displaystyle w^{-\beta}_{CSF}=w_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptw_{CSF}(x,y)={\mathrm{min}}(w_{CSF}(x),w_{CSF}(y)).

Definition 6.3
.

An CSF weight function is a smooth, bounded function
w_{CSF}
on
X
such that

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF;V}})^{k}(\rho_{*}w_{CSF}-r)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{1+\sigma-k})

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF}})^{k}(\rho_{*}w_{CSF}-r)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{1+\sigma})

Set
w^{-\beta}_{CSF}=w_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
and
w_{CSF}(x,y)={\mathrm{min}}(w_{CSF}(x),w_{CSF}(y))
.

Let in the following
(X,g)
be a Riemannian orbifold and

\displaystyle j:{\mathrm{Tub}}_{2\epsilon}(X^{sing})\rightarrow X

be a tubular neighbourhood of the singular stratum of width
\epsilon
. For simplicity we will assume that
X^{sing}=S
is compact and connected.

Definition 6.4
.

A Hermitian vector bundle
E_{CSF},h_{CSF}
is called CSF Hermitian of homogeneous degree
d_{E_{CSF}}
if there exists a family of automorphisms

\displaystyle w^{ACyl}_{CSF;d_{E_{CSF}}}:E_{CSF}\rightarrow E_{CSF}

analogously to
w^{Cyl}_{CF,d_{CF}}
such that

\displaystyle\left(E_{CSF},(w^{ACyl}_{CSF;-d_{E_{CSF}}})^{*}h_{CSF}\right)

is asymptotically cylindrical. A Hermitian bundle
E_{CSF},h_{CSF}
is called CSF Hermitian if it is the sum of CSF Hermitian vector bundle of homogeneous degree. Further a Hermitian connection
\nabla^{h_{CSF}}
on
(E_{CSF},h_{CSF})
is called CSF Hermitian if

\displaystyle(w^{ACyl}_{CSF;d_{T^{*}X\otimes E_{CSF}}})_{*}\nabla^{h_{CSF}}(w^{ACyl}_{CSF;d_{E_{CSF}}})^{*}

is asymptotically cylindrical.

Let
\nabla
be a Hermitian CSF connection. In the presence of the Ehresmann connection
H
the connection
\nabla
decomposes into

\displaystyle\nabla=\nabla^{H;\epsilon}+\nabla^{V;\epsilon}=(\nabla-\chi_{3}\nabla^{CF;H})+(1-\chi_{3})\nabla^{CF;V}

where
\nabla^{CF;V}
is a vertical CF-connection, i.e. it restricts
p
-fibrewise to a connection on
E
. Notice that
\chi_{3}
depends on
\epsilon
.

Remark 41
.

Let
{\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{i,j}(A,B)
denote the sum of all words of length
j
in
A
and
B
counting
i
-times the letter
A
. In particular
{\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{i,j}(A,B)={\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{j-i,j}(B,A)
.

\heartsuit

Definition 6.5
.

Let
(X,g)
be CSF over
q:Y\rightarrow S
and
(E,h,\nabla)
an CSF-bundle. We define the space
\Gamma^{k}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)
to be the space of all
C^{k}
-sections
\Phi
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|w_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}\left\{\nabla^{j}\right\}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}<\infty

We equip the spaces
\Gamma^{k}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)
with the metric

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left|\left|w_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}\left\{\nabla^{j}\right\}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}

The space
\Gamma_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)
is defined to be the intersection of all
\Gamma^{k}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)
for all
k
. Furthermore, we define the
w_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
-weighted Sobolev
W^{k,p}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)
and Hölder spaces
C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)
by replacing the usual metrics by the ones

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|^{p}_{W^{k,p}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\int_{X}|w_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}\left\{\nabla^{i}\right\}\Phi|^{p}_{h,g}w_{CSF;m}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{l}\left|\left|w_{ACF;\beta}\left\{\nabla^{j}\right\}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}+[\nabla^{j}\Phi]_{C^{0,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-j;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle[\nabla^{j}\Phi]_{C^{0,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-j;\epsilon}}=

\displaystyle\underset{B_{g}}{{\mathrm{sup}}}\left\{\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j}w_{CSF;(\beta-j+i-\alpha)}(x,y)\cdot\right.

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\left|{\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{i,j}(\nabla^{H},\nabla^{V})\Phi(x)-\Pi^{x,y}{\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{i,j}(\nabla^{H},\nabla^{V})\Phi(y)\right|_{h,g}}{d_{g}(x,y)^{\alpha}}\right\}

where
B_{g}(U)=\{x,y\in U|\,x\neq y,\,{\mathrm{dist}}_{g}(x,y)\leq w_{ACF;(\beta-j+i-\alpha)}(x,y)\}\subset U\times U
and
\Pi^{x,y}
denotes the parallel transport along the geodesic connecting
x
and
y
.

Remark 42
.

All
W^{k,p}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
,
C^{k}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
and
C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
are Banach spaces,
H^{k}_{CSF;\beta}=W^{k,2}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
is a Hilbert space, but
\Gamma_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}
is not Banach space.

\heartsuit

Theorem 6.2
.

The following holds:

•

Let
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
. The
L^{2}_{CSF}
-pairing induces a perfect pairing

\displaystyle L^{p}_{CSF;\beta}\cong(L^{q}_{CSF;-\beta-m})^{*}

•

If
\beta>\beta^{\prime}
then
W^{l,p}_{CSF;\beta}\subset W^{l,p}_{CSF;\beta^{\prime}}
and
C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta}\subset C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta^{\prime}}

•

If
1\leq p,\infty
then
\Gamma_{CSF;\beta}
is dense in
W^{l,p}_{CF;\beta}

•

(Sobolev, Kondrakov) If
(p_{i},l_{i})\in\mathbb{N}\times[1,\infty)
with
l_{0}\leq l_{1}
such that

\displaystyle l_{0}-\frac{m}{p_{0}}\geq l_{1}-\frac{m}{p_{1}}

and one of the two conditions

(i)

p_{0}\leq p_{1}
and
\beta_{0}\geq\beta_{1}

(ii)

p_{0}>p_{1}
and
\beta_{0}>\beta_{1}

then there exists a continuous embedding

\displaystyle W^{l_{0},p_{0}}_{CSF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow W^{l_{1},p_{1}}_{CSF;\beta_{1}}.

If moreover
l_{0}>l_{1}
,
l_{0}-\frac{m}{p_{0}}>l_{1}-\frac{m}{p_{1}}
and
\beta_{0}>\beta_{1}
then the above embedding is compact, i.e. any bounded sequence in the
(l_{0},p_{0})
norm has a convergent subsequence in the
(l_{1},p_{1})
norm.

•

(Hölder) If
(l_{i},\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,\infty)
and
\beta_{0}<\beta_{1}
with

\displaystyle l_{0}+\alpha_{0}\geq l_{1}+\alpha_{1}

then there exist continuous embeddings

\displaystyle C^{l_{0}+1}_{CSF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{0},\alpha_{0}}_{CSF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{1},\alpha_{1}}_{CSF;\beta_{1}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{1}}_{CSF;\beta_{1}}.

If
\alpha_{1}=0
and
l_{0}=l_{1}
the inclusion
C^{l_{0},\alpha_{0}}_{CSF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{1},\alpha_{1}}_{CSF;\beta_{1}}
is compact.

•

(Morrey, Kondrakov) If
(p_{i},l_{i})\in\mathbb{N}\times[1,\infty)
and
(l,\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}\times[1,\infty)
,
\beta>\delta
, and

\displaystyle l_{0}-\frac{m}{p_{0}}\geq l+\alpha\geq l_{1}-\frac{m}{p_{1}}

then there exists a continuous embedding

\displaystyle W^{l_{0},p_{0}}_{CSF;\beta}\hookrightarrow C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta}\hookrightarrow W^{l_{1},p_{1}}_{CSF;\delta}.

•

Let
(\Phi,\Psi)\mapsto(\phi\cdot\Phi)
is a bilinear form satisfying
|\Phi\cdot\Psi|\lesssim|\Phi||\Psi|
, then

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\cdot\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1}}}\left|\left|\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta}}.

Proof.

These statements follow immediately form the standard Sobolev-Kondrakov-, Hölder- and Morrey-Kondrakov-embedding theorems on compact and on conical spaces
[
AN61
,
ADN64
,
LMO85
,
Loc87
,
Mar02
]
.
∎

6.0.3.
Fredholm Theory for Dirac Operators on Orbifolds

In the following we will establish a Fredholm theory for Dirac operators on weighted function spaces on orbifolds. The Fredholm property will fail at critical rates and wall-crossing formulae are established.

In section
5.3
we established that the operator
\widehat{D}_{0}
can be realised as an isomorphism of weighted function spaces, subject to boundary conditions. In order to show that the orbifold Dirac operator can be realised as a Fredholm map, we will have to compare it to the operator
\widehat{D}_{0}
. The following lemma is proving that close to the singular stratum
D
is approximated by
\widehat{D}_{0}
.

Recall from
3.2
that

\displaystyle D=\widehat{D}_{0}+({\mathrm{cl}}_{g}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}})\circ\nabla^{h}+\mathcal{O}(r).

Definition 6.6
.

We define the restriction map

\displaystyle{\mathrm{res}}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}:C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}(Y,E_{Y},\nabla^{\otimes_{Y}})

\displaystyle\Phi\mapsto{\mathrm{res}}_{CF;\beta}(1-\chi_{3})\Phi

and a section

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ext}}_{CSF}:C^{k,\alpha}(Y,E_{Y})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{loc}(X^{reg},E)

\displaystyle\Phi_{\lambda,\mu}\mapsto(1-\chi_{3}){\mathrm{ext}}_{CF}\Phi_{\lambda,\mu}.

Definition 6.7
.

Define the space
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)={\mathrm{res}}^{-1}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(C^{k+1,\alpha}_{r}(Y,E;+))
.

Lemma 6.1
.

The space
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)
is a Banach space and for
\delta>\beta
and

\displaystyle k+\alpha>k^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}

there exists a compact embedding

(53)

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\hookrightarrow C^{k^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime}}_{CSF;\delta}(X,E).

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
\Phi\in\Gamma^{k+1,\alpha}_{loc}(X,E)
with
{\mathrm{supp}}(\Phi)\subset{\mathrm{Tub}}_{2\epsilon}(S)
. Then

(54)

\displaystyle\left|\left|(D-\widehat{D}_{0})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}\lesssim\epsilon\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}.

Proof.

We use the expansion

\displaystyle D-\widehat{D}_{0}=({\mathrm{cl}}_{g}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}})\circ\nabla^{h}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g}\circ\left(\nabla^{h}-\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}\right).

Notice, that
{\mathrm{cl}}_{g}\circ\left(\nabla^{h}-\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}\right)=\mathcal{O}(r)
is a zeroth order differential operator. The bound follows form the definition of the
C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta}
-norms.
∎

Proposition 6.2

(CSF-Schauder Estimates)
.

Let
\Phi\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)

(55)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

holds.

Proof.

As Schauder estimates are local estimates the estimate holds for all regularity in the “compact region” of the orbifold. Close to the singular stratum we relate the CSF Schauder estimates to the CF-Schauder estimates. As
\left|\left|.\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}
and
\left|\left|.\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}
are equivalent on
{\mathrm{Tub}}_{2\epsilon}(S)
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}_{0}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|(D-\widehat{D}_{0})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\epsilon\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

Thus, by absorbing into the left-hand side we conclude the statement.
∎

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
0>\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
. The map

\displaystyle D:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}(X,E)

is Fredholm and its kernel and cokernel are smooth, i.e. agree for all
\alpha
and
k
. Furthermore, if
\beta_{2}<\beta_{1}
with
|\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}|<1
we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{2}}(D)-{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{1}}(D)=\sum_{\beta_{2}<\lambda<\beta_{1}}d_{\lambda+\frac{m-1}{2}-\delta(E_{0})}(\widehat{D}_{0}).

Proof.

The first statement follows from Proposition
5.2
and
6.2
and goes analogously to the proof of Fredholmness for ACyl, AC or CS operators. Notice, that the Schauder estimate (
6.2
) does not suffices to proof left-semi Fredholmness as the inclusion of

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\hookrightarrow C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E)

is not compact. Thus, we need to improve the Schauder estimate to

(56)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\delta;\epsilon}}

for
\delta>\beta
. Set
\chi_{\varepsilon}=\chi(\varepsilon^{-1}r-1)
. Let us define the operator

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}=D+\chi_{\varepsilon}(\widehat{D}_{0}-D).

This operator is conjugated to
\widehat{D}_{0}
on
{\mathrm{Tub}}_{\varepsilon-e}(S)
and coincides with
D
on the compact part of
X
.

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|(1-\chi_{\varepsilon})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}(1-\chi_{\varepsilon})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

Here we used that
\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}
is conjugate to
\widehat{D}_{0}
on
{\mathrm{Tub}}_{\varepsilon}(S)
and by Proposition
5.2

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{0}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}(X_{0},E_{0})

defines an isomorphism for
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
. Further we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}(1-\chi_{\varepsilon})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|(1-\chi_{\varepsilon})\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|[(1-\chi_{\varepsilon}),\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}]\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|[\chi_{\varepsilon},\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}]\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

Now
[\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e},(1-\chi_{\varepsilon})]=-[\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e},\chi_{\varepsilon}]=\sigma_{\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{\varepsilon})=K_{\varepsilon}
is a compact operator. Further,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-\beta}\left|\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\delta;\epsilon}}

which leads to the following estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon+e}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|K_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\varepsilon^{\delta-\beta}\left|\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\delta;\epsilon}}.

We conclude that the family of operators
\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}
are semi-Fredholm, i.e. have finite dimensional kernel and a closed image. Further, let
P_{\varepsilon}
be a parametrix for the operator
\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}
on
X\backslash{\mathrm{Tub}}_{\varepsilon}(S)
. Define the operator

\displaystyle Q_{\varepsilon}=\chi_{\varepsilon}P_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon+e}+(1-\chi_{\varepsilon})\left(\widehat{D}_{0}\right)^{-1}(1-\chi_{\varepsilon+e}).

Then
\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}Q_{\varepsilon}=1+\widetilde{K}_{\varepsilon}
, where
\widetilde{K}_{\varepsilon}
is compact and hence
\widetilde{D}_{\varepsilon}
is Fredholm. Since
D_{\varepsilon}
converges in the norm-topology and by taking the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
we deduce the Fredholmness of
D
.

For the second statement, we follow
[
Ber23
]
and it suffices to consider the case where
\lambda
is the only critical rate between
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
. Consider the space

\displaystyle V_{\lambda}={\mathrm{ker}}(B_{H;\lambda+\frac{m-1}{2}-\delta(E_{0})}),

the projection
\Pi_{\lambda}:\Gamma(Y,E_{Y};+)\rightarrow V_{\lambda}
, and the space

\displaystyle S_{\beta_{2}}:=\left\{\Phi\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{2};\epsilon}(X,E;+)|D\Phi\in C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1}-1;\epsilon}(X,E)\right\}.

Then for
\Phi\in S_{\beta_{2}}

\displaystyle\Phi-{\mathrm{ext}}_{CSF;\lambda}\circ\Pi_{\lambda}\circ{\mathrm{res}}_{CSF;\lambda}(\Phi)\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1};\epsilon}(X,E;+)

Moreover, we have
S_{\beta_{2}}\subset C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\lambda;\epsilon}(X,E;+)
,
{\mathrm{ker}}_{CSF;\beta_{2}}(D)={\mathrm{ker}}_{\lambda}(D)
, and
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1};\epsilon}(X,E;+)={\mathrm{ker}}(\Pi_{\lambda}\circ{\mathrm{res}}_{CSF;\lambda}:S_{\beta_{2}}\rightarrow V_{\lambda})
as well as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}_{CSF;\beta_{1}}(D)={\mathrm{ker}}\left(\Pi_{\lambda}\circ{\mathrm{res}}_{CSF;\lambda}:{\mathrm{ker}}_{CSF;\beta_{2}}(D)\rightarrow V_{\lambda}\right).

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle S_{\beta_{2}}=C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1};\epsilon}(X,E;+)+{\mathrm{im}}\left({\mathrm{ext}}_{CSF;\lambda}|_{V_{\lambda}}\right)

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}_{CSF;\beta_{2}}(D)=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}\left(D|_{S_{\beta_{2}}}:S_{\beta_{2}}\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1}-1;\epsilon}(X,E)\right)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{coker}}_{CSF;\beta_{2}}(D)=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{coker}}\left(D|_{S_{\beta_{2}}}:S_{\beta_{2}}\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta_{1}-1;\epsilon}(X,E)\right).

The wall-crossing formula follows immediately.
∎

Remark 43
.

Notice, that for
\beta
small enough, the kernel of this map coincides with the kernel of the maps (
51
).

\heartsuit

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
0>\beta\in\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
be the largest rate in
(-\infty,0)
. Then for
\beta<\beta^{\prime}<0

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}(D)={\mathrm{ker}}_{CSF;\beta^{\prime}}(D).

7.
Uniform Elliptic Theory of Adiabatic Families of ACF Dirac Operators

In the following section we will discuss the ACF analysis associated to the family of Dirac operators with torsion on
X_{\zeta}
. These Dirac operators are asymptotic to the operator
D^{\otimes^{t}_{0}}
and a uniform elliptic theory is essential for linear gluing.

7.1.
Weighted Function Spaces on Asymptotically Conical Fibrations

Let in the following
q:(Y,g_{Y})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
be a fibre bundle and a Riemannian submersion of compact Riemannian manifolds. Further, the Riemannian structure on
Y
induces an Ehresmann connection on the fibration, i.e.
TY\cong H_{Y}\oplus VY
.

Definition 7.1
.

A Riemannian fibre bundle
\tilde{p}:(X_{ACF},g_{ACF})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
is asymptotic to a conical fibration (ACF) over a fibration
(q:(Y,g_{Y})\rightarrow(S,g_{S}))
of rate
\gamma
, if there exists compact subset
K_{R}
and a fibre bundle diffeomorphism
\rho:X_{ACF}\backslash K_{R}\rightarrow CF_{S}(E)\backslash B_{R}(E)
such that

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF}})^{k}\left(\rho_{*}g_{ACF}-g_{CF}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{\gamma})

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF}}_{V})^{k}\left(\rho_{*}g_{ACF;V}-g_{CF;V}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{\gamma-k}).

We will further demand that the ACF metric is of the form
g_{ACF}=\tilde{p}^{*}g_{S}+g_{ACF;V}
and hence we equip
p:(X_{ACF},g_{ACF})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
with a connection
H_{ACF}
.

Definition 7.2
.

An ACF weight function is a smooth function
w_{ACF}
on
X_{ACF}
such that

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF;V}})^{k}(\rho_{*}w_{ACF}-r)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{1+\gamma-k})

\displaystyle(\nabla^{g_{CF}})^{k}(\rho_{*}w_{ACF}-r)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{1+\gamma})

Set
w^{-\beta}_{ACF}=w_{ACF;\beta}
and
w_{ACF}(x,y)={\mathrm{min}}(w_{ACF}(x),w_{ACF}(y))
.

Definition 7.3
.

A Hermitian bundle

\displaystyle(E_{ACF},h_{ACF})

is called an ACF Hermitian vector bundle of homogenous degree
|E_{ACF}|
if there exist automorphisms

\displaystyle w^{ACyl}_{ACF;|E_{ACF}|}:E_{ACF}\rightarrow E_{ACF}

analogously defined to
w^{Cyl}_{CF;\delta(E_{0})}
such that

\displaystyle\left(E_{ACF},(w^{ACyl}_{ACF;-|E_{ACF}|})^{*}h_{ACF}\right)

is asymptotically cylindrical. A Hermitian vector bundle is called ACF Hermitian vector bundle if it is the sum of ACF Hermitian vector bundles of homogenous degree. A connection
\nabla^{h_{ACF}}
with respect to
(E_{ACF},h_{ACF})
is called ACF Hermitian connection if

\displaystyle(w^{ACyl}_{ACF;d_{T^{*}X_{ACF}\otimes E_{ACF}}})_{*}\nabla^{h_{ACF}}(w^{ACyl}_{ACF;|E_{ACF}|})^{*}

Is asymptotically cylindrical.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
\nabla
be a Hermitian ACF connection. In the presence of the Ehresmann connection
H
the connection
\nabla
decomposes into

\displaystyle\nabla=\nabla^{H}+\nabla^{V}

where
\nabla^{V}
is a vertical Hermitian connection, i.e. it restricts
p
-fibrewise to a connection on
E
.

Let in the following
\nu_{\zeta}:(X_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
be an ACF space and
(E_{\zeta},h_{\zeta},\nabla_{\zeta})
an ACF Hermitian vector bundle.

Definition 7.4
.

We define the space
\Gamma^{k}_{ACF;\beta}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
to be the space of all
C^{k}
-sections
\Phi
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|w_{ACF;\beta}\left\{\nabla^{j}_{\zeta}\right\}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}<\infty.

We equip the spaces
\Gamma^{k}_{ACF;\beta}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
with the metric

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k}_{ACF;\beta}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left|\left|w_{ACF;\beta}\left\{\nabla^{j}_{\zeta}\right\}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}

The space
\Gamma_{ACF;\beta}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
is defined to be the intersection of all
\Gamma^{k}_{ACF;\beta}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
for all
k
. Furthermore, we define the
w_{ACF;\beta}
-weighted Sobolev
W^{k,p}_{ACF;\beta}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
and Hölder spaces
C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
by replacing the usual metrics by the ones

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|^{p}_{W^{k,p}_{ACF;\beta}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{I=0}^{k}\int_{X}|w_{ACF;\beta}\left\{\nabla^{i}_{\zeta}\right\}\Phi|^{p}_{h,g}w_{ACF;m}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{l}\left|\left|w_{ACF;\beta}\left\{\nabla^{j}_{\zeta}\right\}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}}+[\nabla^{j}_{\zeta}\Phi]_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-j}}

\displaystyle[\nabla^{j}\Phi]_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-j}}=

\displaystyle\underset{B_{g_{\zeta}}}{{\mathrm{sup}}}\left\{\bigoplus_{i=0}^{j}w_{ACF;(\beta-j+i-\alpha)}(x,y)\cdot\right.

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\left|{\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{i,j}(\nabla^{H}_{\zeta},\nabla^{V}_{\zeta})\Phi(x)-\Pi^{x,y}{\mathrm{word}}^{+}_{i,j}(\nabla^{H}_{\zeta},\nabla^{V}_{\zeta})\Phi(y)\right|_{h,g}}{d_{g_{\zeta}}(x,y)^{\alpha}}\right\}

where
B_{g_{\zeta}}(U)=\{x,y\in U|\,x\neq y,\,{\mathrm{dist}}_{g_{\zeta}}(x,y)\leq w_{ACF;(\beta-j+i-\alpha)}(x,y)\}\subset U\times U
and
\Pi^{x,y}
denotes the parallel transport along the geodesic connecting
x
and
y
.

Remark 44
.

All
W^{k,p}_{ACF;\beta}
,
\Gamma^{k}_{ACF;\beta}
and
C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}
are Banach spaces,
H^{k}_{ACF;\beta}=W^{k,2}_{ACF;\beta}
is an Hilbert space, but
\Gamma_{ACF;\beta}
is not an Banach space.

\heartsuit

Theorem 7.1
.

The following holds:

•

Let
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
. The
L^{2}_{ACF}
-pairing induces a perfect pairing

\displaystyle L^{p}_{ACF;\beta}\cong(L^{q}_{ACF;-\beta-m})^{*}

•

If
\beta>\beta^{\prime}
then
W^{l,p}_{ACF;\beta}\subset W^{l,p}_{ACF;\beta^{\prime}}
and
C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}\subset C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta^{\prime}}

•

If
1\leq p,\infty
then
\Gamma_{CSF;\beta}
is dense in
W^{l,p}_{CF;\beta}

•

(Sobolev, Kondrakov) If
(p_{i},l_{i})\in\mathbb{N}\times[1,\infty)
with
l_{0}\leq l_{1}
such that

\displaystyle l_{0}-\frac{m}{p_{0}}\geq l_{1}-\frac{m}{p_{1}}

and one of the two conditions

(i)

p_{0}\leq p_{1}
and
\beta_{0}\geq\beta_{1}

(ii)

p_{0}>p_{1}
and
\beta_{0}>\beta_{1}

then there exists a continuous embedding

\displaystyle W^{l_{0},p_{0}}_{ACF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow W^{l_{1},p_{1}}_{ACF;\beta_{1}}.

If moreover
l_{0}>l_{1}
,
l_{0}-\frac{m}{p_{0}}>l_{1}-\frac{m}{p_{1}}
and
\beta_{0}>\beta_{1}
then the above embedding is compact, i.e. any bounded sequence in the
(l_{0},p_{0})
norm has a convergent subsequence in the
(l_{1},p_{1})
norm.

•

(Hölder) If
(l_{i},\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}\times[0,\infty)
and
\beta_{0}<\beta_{1}
with

\displaystyle l_{0}+\alpha_{0}\geq l_{1}+\alpha_{1}

then there exist continuous embeddings

\displaystyle C^{l_{0}+1}_{ACF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{0},\alpha_{0}}_{ACF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{1},\alpha_{1}}_{ACF;\beta_{1}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{1}}_{ACF;\beta_{1}}.

If
\alpha_{1}=0
and
l_{0}=l_{1}
the inclusion
C^{l_{0},\alpha_{0}}_{ACF;\beta_{0}}\hookrightarrow C^{l_{1},\alpha_{1}}_{ACF;\beta_{1}}
is compact.

•

(Morrey, Kondrakov) If
(p_{i},l_{i})\in\mathbb{N}\times[1,\infty)
and
(l,\alpha)\in\mathbb{N}\times[1,\infty)
,
\beta>\delta
, and

\displaystyle l_{0}-\frac{m}{p_{0}}\geq l+\alpha\geq l_{1}-\frac{m}{p_{1}}

then there exists a continuous embedding

\displaystyle W^{l_{0},p_{0}}_{ACF;\beta}\hookrightarrow C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}\hookrightarrow W^{l_{1},p_{1}}_{ACF;\delta}.

•

Let
(\Phi,\Psi)\mapsto(\phi\cdot\Phi)
is a bilinear form satisfying
|\Phi\cdot\Psi|\lesssim|\Phi||\Psi|
, then

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\cdot\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1}}}\left|\left|\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{l,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}}.

Proof.

These statements follow immediately form the standard Sobolev-Kondrakov-, Hölder- and Morrey-Kondrakov-embedding theorems on compact and on conical spaces
[
AN61
,
ADN64
,
LMO85
,
Loc87
,
Mar02
]
.
∎

Remark 45
.

Given a weight function
w_{\zeta}
for
(X_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},g_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})
we define new weight functions on
(X_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta})
by

\displaystyle w_{ACF;t}:=\delta_{t}^{*}w_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}=t\cdot w_{\zeta}=t\cdot w_{ACF}.

\heartsuit

7.2.
Uniform Fredholm Theory for Adiabatic Families of Asymptotically Conically Fibred Dirac Operators

In the following section we will address the Fredholm analysis of the non-compact ACF-family of Dirac bundles, modelling the resolution locally. By the results of the prior section we know that the following is true.

Lemma 7.2
.

The map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}:{\mathrm{dom}}_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})

is unbounded. We define

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})

as the domain, equipped with the graph norm.

The goal of the following section is to show, that the map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})

is Fredholm, and that there exists adapted norms such that
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
admits a uniformally bounded right-inverse.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
\Phi\in\Gamma^{k+1,\alpha}_{loc}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
with
{\mathrm{supp}}(\Phi)\subset X_{\zeta}\backslash B_{2t^{-1}\epsilon}(X_{\zeta})
. Then

(57)

\displaystyle\left|\left|(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{0})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}\lesssim t^{-\eta-1}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}.

Proof.

We use the expansion

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}-\widehat{D}_{0}=({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{0}})\circ\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}\circ\left(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}-\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}\right).

Notice, that
{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}\circ\left(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}-\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}\right)=\mathcal{O}(r^{1-\eta})
is a zeroth order differential operator. The bound follows form the definition of the
C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}
-norms.
∎

Proposition 7.1

(ACF Schauder Estimates)
.

Let
\Phi\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})

(58)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

holds.

Proof.

We will prove (
7.1
) analogously to the proof of (
5.1
). Let
\mathcal{U}_{\epsilon}
denote a good cover of
X_{\zeta}
by geodesic balls of radius
\epsilon
and let
\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{\epsilon}
denote the cover of
X_{\zeta}
by opens of the form

\displaystyle\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}U\cong B_{\epsilon}(s)\times M_{\zeta(s)}.

We can do this in a way such that for each
0<c
there exists and
0<\epsilon
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|g^{t}_{\zeta}-g_{S}\oplus t^{2}\cdot g_{\zeta(s)}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{t}}<c

holds on
\mathcal{U}_{\epsilon}
. As
g^{t}_{\zeta}
is uniformally close to the product structures on
\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}\mathcal{U}_{\epsilon}
, so is the Hermitian Dirac bundle structure. By now rescaling the balls and by using
[
Wal17a
, Prop. 7.7]
and
[
Wal17a
, Prop. 7.11]
we deduce the standard Schauder estimates.

Higher regularity automatically follows from the CF-Schauder estimate and the fact that
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\sim\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}

∎

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
. The map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})

is Fredholm and its kernel and cokernel are smooth, i.e. agree for all
\alpha
and
k
. Furthermore, if
\beta_{2}>\beta_{1}
with
|\beta_{2}-\beta_{1}|<1
we have

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{1}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})-{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})=\sum_{\beta_{2}<\lambda<\beta_{1}}d_{\lambda+\frac{m-1}{2}-\delta(E_{0})}(\widehat{D}_{0}).

Proof.

The first statement follows from Proposition
5.2
and
6.2
and goes analogously to the proof of Fredholmness for ACyl, AC or CS operators. Notice, that the Schauder estimate (
6.2
) does not suffices to proof left-semi Fredholmness as the inclusion of

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\hookrightarrow C^{0}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})

is not compact. Thus, we need to improve the Schauder estimate to

(59)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{ACF;\delta;t}}

for
\delta>\beta
. Let us define the operator

\displaystyle\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon}=\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}+(1-\chi^{t}_{3})(\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}).

This operator is conjugated to
\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}
on
X_{\zeta}\backslash{\mathrm{Tub}}_{3\epsilon}(S)
and coincides with
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
on the “compact” part of
X_{\zeta}
.

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon}\chi^{t}_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

Here we used that
\widetilde{D}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}
is conjugate to
\widehat{D}_{0}
on
X_{\zeta}\backslash{\mathrm{Tub}}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}(S)
and by Proposition
5.2

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{0}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(\widehat{D}_{0};+)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}(X_{0},E_{0})

defines an isomorphism for
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
. Further we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|[(1-\chi^{t}_{3}),\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}]\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}\chi^{t}_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{3}\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|[\chi^{t}_{3},\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}]\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

Now
[\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon},(1-\chi^{t}_{3})]=-[\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon},\chi^{t}_{3}]=\sigma_{\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3})=K^{t}_{\varepsilon}
is a compact operator. Further,

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{\delta-\beta}\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{ACF;\delta;t}}

which leads to the following estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon+\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|K^{t}_{\varepsilon}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+t^{\delta-\beta}\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\delta;\epsilon}}.

We conclude that the family of operators
\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon}
are semi-Fredholm, i.e. have finite dimensional kernel and a closed image. Further, let
P^{t}_{2\epsilon}
be a parametrix for the operator
\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon}
on
X_{\zeta}\backslash{\mathrm{Tub}}_{3\epsilon}(S)
. Define the operator

\displaystyle Q^{t}_{\epsilon}=(1-\chi^{t}_{3})P^{t}_{2\epsilon}(1-\chi^{t}_{3\epsilon-\varepsilon})+\chi^{t}_{3}\left(\widehat{D}_{0}\right)^{-1}\chi_{3\epsilon-\varepsilon}.

Then
\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon}Q^{t}_{\epsilon}=1+\widetilde{K}^{t}_{\varepsilon}
, where
\widetilde{K}_{\varepsilon}
is compact and hence
\widetilde{D}^{t}_{3\epsilon}
is Fredholm. Taking the limit
\varepsilon\to\infty
proves the statement.

For the second statement, we follow
[
Ber23
]
and it suffices to consider the case where
\lambda
is the only critical rate between
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
. Consider the space

\displaystyle V_{\lambda}={\mathrm{ker}}(B_{H;\lambda+\frac{m-1}{2}-\delta(E_{0})}),

the projection
\Pi_{\lambda}:\Gamma(Y,E_{Y};+)\rightarrow V_{\lambda}
, and the space

\displaystyle S_{\beta_{2}}:=\left\{\Phi\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{2};t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\in C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1}-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})\right\}.

Then for
\Phi\in S_{\beta_{2}}

\displaystyle\Phi-{\mathrm{ext}}_{ACF;\lambda;t}\circ\Pi_{\lambda}\circ{\mathrm{res}}_{ACF;\lambda;t}(\Phi)\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1};t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})

Moreover, we have
S_{\beta_{2}}\subset C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\lambda;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})
,
{\mathrm{ker}}_{ACF;\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})={\mathrm{ker}}_{\lambda}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})
, and
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1};t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})={\mathrm{ker}}(\Pi_{\lambda}\circ{\mathrm{res}}_{ACF;\lambda;t}:S_{\beta_{2}}\rightarrow V_{\lambda})
as well as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}_{ACF;\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})={\mathrm{ker}}\left(\Pi_{\lambda}\circ{\mathrm{res}}_{ACF;\lambda;t}:{\mathrm{ker}}_{ACF;\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow V_{\lambda}\right).

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle S_{\beta_{2}}=C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1};t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})+{\mathrm{im}}\left({\mathrm{ext}}_{ACF;\lambda;t}|_{V_{\lambda}}\right)

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}_{ACF;\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}|_{S_{\beta_{2}}}:S_{\beta_{2}}\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1}-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})\right)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{coker}}_{ACF;\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{coker}}\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}|_{S_{\beta_{2}}}:S_{\beta_{2}}\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta_{1}-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})\right).

The wall-crossing formula follows immediately.
∎

Notice, that the parametrix is not uniformally bounded in
t
and hence not suitable for further use in gluing analysis. The following section is devoted to carefully analysing the family
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
and to constructed adapted norms that allow uniform bounds on a right-inverse of
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
.

7.2.1.
Pushforwards of ACF Dirac Bundles and Concentrating Dirac Operators

Using the projection
\nu_{\zeta}:X_{\zeta}\rightarrow S
and the Ehresmann connection
H_{\zeta}
we define the
C^{\infty}_{ACF;\beta}
-pushforward of the Clifford module
(E_{\zeta},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}})
on
S
.

Notice that even though
C^{\infty}_{loc}
-sections of
E_{\zeta}
define a sheaf
\mathcal{E}_{\zeta}
of Fréchet spaces on
X_{\zeta}
; the weighted
C^{\infty}_{ACF;\beta}
-sections only form a presheaf, by the lack of gluing. Nevertheless,
\nu_{\zeta}
-pushforward of
C^{\infty}_{ACF;\beta;t}
-defines a coherent, locally free sheave of
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*}C^{\infty}_{ACyl}
-modules

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*}\mathcal{E}_{\zeta;ACF;\beta;t}

on
S
. This sheave can be identified with the sheave of
C^{\infty}
-sections of the infinite dimensional Frechét vector bundle

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}.

In a similar way we define the
H^{\infty}_{ACF}
-pushforward

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{H^{\infty}_{ACF}}}E_{\zeta}.

Lemma 7.4
.

The
C^{\infty}_{ACF;\beta}
-pushforward of
E_{\zeta}
can be endowed with the structure of an infinite-dimensional Fréchet Dirac bundle. The
H^{\infty}_{ACF}
-pushforward can be endowed with the structure of an infinite-dimensional Hermitian Fréchet Dirac bundle.

Proof.

We define a
{\mathrm{Cl}}(TS,g_{S})
-module structures on
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}
by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S}}(V)\Phi={\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}(V^{H_{\zeta}})\Phi={\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(V^{H_{\zeta}})\Phi

for vector fields
V\in\mathfrak{X}(S)
. Furthermore the connection
\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}
induces a connection on
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}
by

\displaystyle\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}_{V}\Phi=\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}_{V^{H_{\zeta}}}\Phi,

whose curvature is given by

\displaystyle F_{\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}}(U,V)=F_{\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}}(U^{H_{\zeta}},V^{H_{\zeta}})-\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}_{F_{H_{\zeta}}(U,V)}.

In a similar way, we define the vector bundle

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{H^{\infty}_{ACF}}}E_{\zeta}

and equip this bundle with a Dirac bundle structure analogously to the above. Naturally the
H^{\infty}_{ACF}
-pushforward is equipped with Hermitian structure

\displaystyle h^{t}_{\zeta}(\Phi,\Phi^{\prime})_{s}=\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left<\Phi,\Phi^{\prime}\right>_{h^{t}_{\zeta}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta;V}}

given by the fibrewise
L^{2}_{t}
-product. Even though the connection
\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}
fails to be a Hermitian connection, the connection

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}=\nabla^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}}-\tfrac{1}{2}k_{\zeta}.

is Hermitian. In particular the latter is a
{\mathrm{Cl}}(TS,g^{S})
-module connection and
thus

\displaystyle((\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S}},h^{t}_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}})

is an infinite dimensional Fréchet Dirac bundle and

\displaystyle((\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{H^{\infty}_{ACF}}}E_{\zeta},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S}},h^{t}_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}})

is an infinite dimensional Hermitian Dirac bundle.

∎

Notice further, that a grading
\epsilon_{\zeta}
on
(E_{\zeta},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}},h_{\zeta},\nabla^{h_{\zeta}})
induces a grading on both the
C^{\infty}_{ACF;\beta}
and the
L^{2}_{ACF}
-pushforward.

Instead of viewing the Dirac Operator
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
as an operator

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}:\Gamma_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow\Gamma_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})

we will now consider it as an operator

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta}:{\mathrm{dom}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\subset\Gamma(S,(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta})\rightarrow\Gamma(S,(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta-1}}E_{\zeta})

Lemma 7.5
.

The horizontal Dirac operator defines a Dirac operator

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta}:\Gamma(S,(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta})\rightarrow\Gamma(S,(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}).

Proof.

The horizontal Dirac operator
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}
as established in section
3.3
is given by

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}={\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}\circ{\mathrm{pr}}_{H_{\zeta}}\circ\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes_{\zeta}}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\tau_{\zeta;H}^{t})+t^{-1}\cdot{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\tau^{t}_{\zeta;V})-\frac{t}{4}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(F_{H_{\zeta}}-\rho^{*}_{\zeta}F_{H_{0}}).

Its
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}
-pushforward is a first order differential operator on sections of
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}
whose symbol is given by

\displaystyle\sigma_{\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta}}(\xi)={\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\xi^{H})={\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S}}(\xi)

and hence is a Dirac operator.
∎

By the previous section, we know that the operator
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
is of the form

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}=\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}+t^{-1}\cdot D_{\zeta;V}

and we notice that the vertical Dirac operator
D_{\zeta;V}\in\Gamma(S,{\mathrm{End}}((\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{L^{2}}}E_{\zeta}))
and hence
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
is family of concentrating Dirac operators
7
7

7

A family of Dirac operator is called concentrating if
D^{t}=D+t^{-1}\cdot\Phi
for
\Phi\in\Gamma(S,{\mathrm{End}}(E_{S})
(see
[
Par23
]
. The idea of Bismut in
[
Bis86
]
was that the behaviour of
D^{t}_{\zeta}
is determined by the operator that concentrates on the kernel of the vertical Dirac operator. In the following we will make these idea concrete in the subject case. Notice that a major difference between Bismuts construction and ours is that the fibres of
X_{\zeta}
are noncompact.

Remark 46
.

The vertical Dirac operator defines bounded linear maps

\displaystyle D_{\zeta;V}:

\displaystyle W^{k+1,p}_{AC;\beta}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta})\rightarrow W^{k,p}_{AC;\beta-1}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta})

\displaystyle D_{\zeta;V}:

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta}).

These maps are Fredholm as long as
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
and by elliptic regularity their kernels are independent of
k
. Furthermore, the indices of these operators satisfy the wall-crossing formula

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{1}}\left(D_{\zeta;V}\right)-{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{2}}\left(D_{\zeta;V}\right)=\sum_{\beta_{1}<\beta<\beta_{2}}d_{\beta}(D_{0;V})

\heartsuit

Definition 7.5
.

We define the splitting

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}\cong\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\oplus\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})

into the kernel and coimage of
D_{\zeta;V}
as well as the splitting of

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta-1}}E_{\zeta}\cong\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\oplus\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})

into cokernel and image of
D_{0;V}
.

As long as
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
, both

and
\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
are finite dimensional, the former remains finite dimensional even when

. In particular, the vertical Dirac operator defines an isomorphism

\displaystyle D_{\zeta;V}:\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\xrightarrow{\cong}\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}).

Assumption 4
.

Let assume that there exist vector bundles
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})

.

This in particular, implies that for
\beta_{1}>\beta_{2}
there exists an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta_{2}}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\\

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta_{1}}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\\
\ominus\\
\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta_{1}}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{\beta_{2}<\beta<\beta_{1}}\mathcal{L}_{\beta}(D_{0;V})}}

In section
6
we proved that the vector bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\beta}(D_{0;V})
coincides with

.

Proof.

. It suffices to consider the case where

Then for

Remark 47
.

If
\beta<-\frac{m}{2}
, then there exists a compact embedding

and hence both
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
and

are Hermitian for all

Remark 48
.

Remark 49
.

Lets briefly imagine that the fibres of
X_{\zeta}
where compact. Let in the following assume that if

is even the vertical Dirac operator

In this case the index of the family of Dirac operators

if

if

\displaystyle[{\mathrm{Ind}}(D_{\zeta;V})]

\displaystyle\in K^{1}(s)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{If }m\equiv 1\,{\mathrm{mod}}2

Under a small generic perturbation

.

.

The operator

Lemma 7.7
.

The family of Dirac bundle structure of
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}
and
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta-1}}E_{\zeta}
restrict to Dirac bundle structures on

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}),\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}),\hskip 14.22636pt\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}).

Moreover, the induced Hermitian structure is Hermitian Dirac.

We decompose the Dirac operator into

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{II}}&\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{KI}}\\
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}&\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\\
\end{array}\right)

according to the decomposition of the pushforward of the Dirac bundle.

Lemma 7.8
.

If
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}
and
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;V}
anti-commute the off-diagonal terms

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{KI}}:

\displaystyle\Gamma(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))\rightarrow\Gamma(S,\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}:

\displaystyle\Gamma(S,\mathcal{C}\mathcal{I}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))\rightarrow\Gamma(S,\mathcal{C}\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

vanish.

Proof.

We can rewrite the off-diagonal terms as

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{KI}}=

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{V;-\beta-m-1}D_{\zeta;V}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{V;-\beta-m-1}\{D_{\zeta;V},\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}=

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}D_{\zeta;V}L_{V;-\beta-m-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\{\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H},D_{\zeta;V}\}L_{V;-\beta-m-1}

∎

7.2.2.
Vertically Harmonic Sections on Vertically Scalar-Flat Fibrations

Let in the following assume that
\nu_{\zeta}:(X_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
is fibrewise scalar-flat and is ACF of rate
\gamma=\eta=-m
.

The following Lemma proves that if the vertical kernel bundles exists, the fibres are given by smooth elements that decay at least by a power of
1-m
, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})=\mathcal{K}_{L^{2}}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})

for
\beta\in(1-m,0)
.

Lemma 7.9
.

The fibres of the vertical kernels coincide with the kernels of

\displaystyle D_{\zeta;V}(s):C^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta(s)})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta(s)})

independent of
0<\alpha<1
,
k
and
\beta\in[1-m,0)
. In particular when
\beta<-m/2
then
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}\cong\mathcal{K}_{L^{2}}
by Theorem
7.1
.

By the elliptic regularity of uniform elliptic asymptotically conical operator
[
Mar02
]
first part of the statement holds. To prove the independence in
\beta
we need the following result.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
g_{V,\zeta}|_{M_{\zeta}}
be scalar-flat and let
\Phi\in\Gamma(\nu^{-1}_{\zeta}(s),E_{\zeta})
. If
D_{\zeta;V}\Phi=0
and
\Phi=o(1)
then
\nabla^{k}\Phi=\mathcal{O}(r^{1-m-k})
.

Proof.

We will prove this statement similar to the proof of Proposition 5.10 in
[
Wal17a
]
. Notice that
D_{\zeta;V}
is a Dirac operator and hence the Kato inequality

(62)

\displaystyle|{\mathrm{d}}|\Phi||\leq\sqrt{\frac{m-1}{m}}|\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}\Phi|

holds on the complement of the vanishing locus of
\Phi
. Now using the Weitzenböck formula

\displaystyle(D_{\zeta;V})^{2}=(\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}})^{*}\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{VV}(F^{tw.}_{\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}}),

|F^{tw.}_{\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}}|=\mathcal{O}(r^{-m})
, and the identity

\displaystyle\Delta^{g_{V,\zeta}}|\Phi|^{2}+2|\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}\Phi|^{2}=2\left<(\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}})^{*}\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}\Phi,\Phi\right>

we obtain the following estimate on the complement of the vanishing locus of
\Phi

\displaystyle\frac{2(m-1)}{m-2}\Delta^{g_{V,\zeta}}|\Phi|^{\frac{m-2}{m-1}}\leq

\displaystyle|\Phi|^{-\frac{m+1}{m}}\left(\Delta^{g_{V,\zeta}}|\Phi|^{2}+\frac{2m}{m-1}|{\mathrm{d}}|\Phi||^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Phi|^{-\frac{m}{m-1}}\left(\Delta^{g_{V,\zeta}}|\Phi|^{2}+2|\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}\Phi|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2|\Phi|^{-\frac{m}{m-1}}\left<(\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}})^{*}\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}\Phi,\Phi\right>

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|\Phi|^{-\frac{m}{m-1}}\left(\left<(D_{\zeta;V})^{*}D_{\zeta;V}\Phi,\Phi\right>+\left<{\mathrm{cl}}_{VV}(F_{\nabla^{E_{\zeta;V}}})\Phi,\Phi\right>\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}(r^{-m})|\Phi|^{\frac{m-2}{m-1}}

Now let
f=|\Phi|^{\frac{m-2}{m-1}}
on
(V_{\Phi})^{c}=\{m\in M_{\zeta}|\psi(m)\neq 0\}
. By the above

\displaystyle\Delta^{g_{V,\zeta}}f\lesssim f\cdot w_{AC;m}.

Since
f
is bounded, by
[
Joy00
, Theorem 8.3.6(a)]
8
8

8

Here we used a Sobolev embedding of
W^{2,p}\hookrightarrow C^{0,\alpha}
for big enough
p>1
.
there exists a
g=\mathcal{O}(r^{2-m})
such that

\displaystyle\Delta g=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(\Delta^{g_{V,\zeta}}f)^{+}&\text{on }(V_{\Phi})^{c}\\
0&V_{\Phi}\end{array}\right.

where
(.)^{+}
denotes the positive part. As
g
is superharmonic and decays at infinity, the maximum principle implies its nonnegativity. Further, the function
f-g
is subharmonic on
(V_{\Phi})^{c}
, decays at infinity and is nonnegative on the boundary of
(V_{\Phi})^{c}
. Again using the maximum principle
f\leq g=\mathcal{O}(r^{2-m})
and we deduce that

\displaystyle|\Phi|=\mathcal{O}(r^{1-m}).

∎

Remark 51
.

From now on we assume that there exists a critical rate
\mu_{\mathcal{K}}\in(-\infty,1-m]
such that the kernels

\displaystyle D_{\zeta;V}(s):C^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta})

are isomorphic for all
\beta\in[\mu_{\mathcal{K}},0)
. This in particular, implies that for
\beta_{2}>\beta_{1}>\mu_{\mathcal{K}}
there exists an exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta_{1}}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta_{2}}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\bigoplus_{\beta_{1}<\beta<\beta_{2}}\mathcal{L}_{\beta}(D_{0;V})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

\heartsuit

Example 7
.

Let
D_{\zeta}={\mathrm{d}}\oplus{\mathrm{d}}^{*_{g_{\zeta}}}
. The vector bundle of vertical kernels will always exist and will be refereed to as the vector bundle of vertical harmonic forms. In particular, we identify

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}X_{\zeta}}/\nu_{\zeta})\cong\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}S\otimes\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S)

In the case
(X_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta})
is given by Example
1

[
Loc87
]
and
[
Joy00
]
ensure the existence of an isomorphism of flat vector bundles

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbb{R}}\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S)\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S).

\clubsuit

Lemma 7.10
.

Let
X_{\zeta}
be given by Example
1
with
m=4
,
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(2)
and

\displaystyle(E,{\mathrm{cl}}_{g},h,\nabla^{h})=(\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}X,{\mathrm{cl}}_{g},g,\nabla^{g}).

Then
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\cong\wedge^{\bullet}T^{*}S\otimes\mathcal{H}^{2}_{-}(X_{\zeta}/S)
and
\mu_{\mathcal{K}}=-4
.

Proof.

In some cases we can even further improve the Kato inequality (
62
). The only vertical harmonic forms on
X_{\zeta}
are anti-selfdual two forms. Following the results of Walter Seaman
[
Sea91
]
we improve the Kato inequality to

(63)

\displaystyle|{\mathrm{d}}|\Phi||\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}|\nabla^{g_{\zeta;V}}\Phi|.

This is achieved by a closer inspection of the Weitzenböck-curvature. Then by following the arguments of the proof of Proposition
7.2
with
|\Phi|^{2/3}
being replace by
|\Phi|^{1/2}
we deduce the statement.
∎

7.2.3.
The Adiabatic Residues, Adiabatic Kernels and Cokernels

In the following we will construct an elliptic first order differential operator on sections of
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
and
\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
, which we will refer to as the adiabatic residue of
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
. This operator will be used as a model operator for
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
. The idea of considering this operator as a model operator builds up on the work of Bismut in
[
Bis86
,
BL95
,
BC89
]
, generalising the work of Walpuski in
[
Wal17a
,
Wal17b
]
and Platt in
[
Pla20
]
. This operator will be the right model operator to consider for understanding uniform elliptic theory on
(X^{t},g^{t})
as it encaptures the geometric structure of the singular strata in contrast to a more standard approach to construct a model operator by using
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
for the flat metric
g_{st}
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proposition 7.3
.

We define the effective kernel operator and the effective cokernel operators

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}^{t}=

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}^{t}=

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}

These operators are formally self-adjoint, elliptic, first order differential operators that are given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}^{t}=

\displaystyle D_{\mathcal{K};\beta}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S};\mathcal{K}}((\tau^{t}_{\zeta;H})_{\mathcal{K};\beta})+t^{-1}\cdot{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\tau^{t}_{\zeta;V})_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle-\frac{t}{4}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S};\mathcal{K};\beta}({\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(F_{H_{\zeta}}-\rho_{\zeta}^{*}F_{H_{0}})_{\mathcal{K};\beta}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}^{t}=

\displaystyle D_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S};\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}((\tau^{t}_{\zeta;H})_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta})+t^{-1}\cdot{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\tau^{t}_{\zeta;V})_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle-\frac{t}{4}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{S};\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}({\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(F_{H_{\zeta}}-\rho_{\zeta}^{*}F_{H_{0}})_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}),

whereby

\displaystyle D_{\mathcal{K};\beta}:

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow\Gamma\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)

\displaystyle D_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}:

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow\Gamma\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)

is the Dirac-operator associated to the Dirac bundle
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
and
\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
. The other terms appearing are endomorphisms of the former two. The limits

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}=D_{\mathcal{K};\beta}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\dot{\tau}^{0}_{\zeta;V})_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle\lim_{t\to 0}\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}=D_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\zeta}}(\dot{\tau}^{0}_{\zeta;V})_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}

will be referred to as the adiabatic residues of
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
. Here
\dot{\tau}^{0}_{\zeta;V}=\left.\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}t}\tau^{t}_{\zeta;V}\right|_{t=0}
.

Corollary 7.1
.

If
(E_{\zeta},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}},h^{t}_{\zeta},\nabla^{h^{t}_{\zeta}})
is graded, so are all the constructed structures.

Remark 52
.

In
[
Goe14
]
the adiabatic residue is referred to as the effective horizontal operator.

\heartsuit

Example 8
.

Let
(X_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta})
satisfy

(64)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbb{R}}\oplus\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S)\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S).

The adiabatic residue of the Hodge-de Rham operator on is given by the self-adjoint, elliptic, first order differential operator

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{GM}:\bigoplus_{p+q=\bullet}\Omega^{p}\left(S,\mathcal{H}^{q}(X_{\zeta}/S)\right)\rightarrow\bigoplus_{p+q=\bullet}\Omega^{p}\left(S,\mathcal{H}^{q}(X_{\zeta}/S)\right)

given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{GM}={\mathrm{d}}_{GM}\oplus{\mathrm{d}}_{GM}^{*}.

The Dirac operator

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{GM}\oplus{\mathrm{d}}_{GM}^{*}

associated to the vertical kernel Clifford bundle, is given by the Gauß-Manin-Hodge-de Rham operator, i.e. the Hodge-de Rham operator twisted by the flat Gauß-Manin connection on
\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S)\rightarrow S
.

\clubsuit

Proposition 7.4
.

The analytic realisation of the adiabatic residues, seen as a bounded maps

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}:

\displaystyle W^{k+1,p}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow W^{k,p}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}:

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}:

\displaystyle W^{k+1,p}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow W^{k,p}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}:

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)

are Fredholm. Elliptic regularity shows that the kernel and cokernels of these consists of smooth sections. Moreover, we have

(65)

\displaystyle\left|\left|(\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}-\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta})\phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t\left|\left|\phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

(66)

\displaystyle\left|\left|(\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}-\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta})\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t\left|\left|\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

Notice, that by the above and the fact that
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathfrak{K};\beta}
and
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}
are bounded from below of the complement of their kernels, that for small
t

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}(\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{K};\beta})\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{ker}}(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta})

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{coker}}(\mathfrak{D}^{t}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta})\hookrightarrow{\mathrm{coker}}(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}).

Definition 7.7
.

We will refer to the space

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Ker}_{ACF;\beta}(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta})={\mathrm{ker}}\left(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\right)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathfrak{CoKer}_{ACF;\beta}(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta})={\mathrm{coker}}\left(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\right).

as the adiabatic kernel and adiabatic cokernel.

Definition 7.8
.

The Dirac operator
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
will be called isentropic, if

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Ker}_{ACF;\beta}\left(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta}\right)\cong{\mathrm{ker}}_{ACF;\beta}\left(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta}\right)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\mathfrak{CoKer}_{ACF;\beta}(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta})\cong{\mathrm{coker}}_{ACF;\beta}\left(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta}\right)

Remark 53
.

An isentropic process in statistical mechanics is a process that is adiabatic and reversible. Usually information about the Dirac operator is lost in the adiabatic limit, but in the case of isentropic operators, an element in the kernel of the adiabatic residue determines an element in the kernel of Dirac operator for
t\neq 0
.

\heartsuit

Lemma 7.11

(Adiabatic Wall-Crossing)
.

Let
\beta_{1}>\beta_{2}
no critical rates. Then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Ind}_{\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta})-\mathfrak{Ind}_{\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta})=\sum_{\beta_{2}<\lambda<\beta_{1}}d_{\lambda+\frac{m-1}{2}-\delta(E_{0})}(\widehat{D}_{0})

Proof.

This immediately follows from Lemma
7.6
and
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\sim\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}
.
∎

Remark 54
.

Let
(X_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta})
be as in Example
8
. We can view the adiabatic residue of the Hodge-de Rham operator as the elliptic operator associated to the local system of

\displaystyle L^{2}GM^{\bullet}(\nu_{\zeta}:X_{\zeta}\rightarrow S)=(\Omega^{\bullet}(S,\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S)),{\mathrm{d}}_{GM}).

Thus, the kernel of the of the adiabatic residue of
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
can be identified with the cohomology

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(L^{2}GM^{\bullet}(\nu_{\zeta}:X_{\zeta}\rightarrow S)).

If
\pi_{1}(S)=0
then Hurwitz-formula holds for the cohomology of
X_{\zeta}
. Furthermore, if
\pi_{p}(S)=0
the above coincides with the group cohomology

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}^{p}(L^{2}GM^{\bullet}(\nu_{\zeta}:X_{\zeta}\rightarrow S))={\mathrm{H}}^{p}(\pi_{1}(S),\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(M_{\zeta(s)}))

of the monodromy representation

\displaystyle\rho_{GM}:\pi_{1}(S)\rightarrow\bigtimes_{q=0}^{m}{\mathrm{GL}}(\mathcal{H}^{q}(M_{\zeta(s)})).

\heartsuit

Furthermore, standard results from algebraic topology link the adiabatic kernel of the Hodge-de Rham operator to the Leray-Serre spectral sequence.

Theorem 7.3

(
[
BT+82
]
)
.

There exists a spectral sequence
E^{p,q}_{N}
associated to the double complex

\displaystyle(K^{\bullet_{1},\bullet_{2}},{\mathrm{d}})=(\check{C}^{\bullet_{1}}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}\mathcal{U},\Omega^{\bullet_{2}}),\delta+(-1)^{\bullet_{1}}{\mathrm{d}}_{dR})

converging to the cohomology of
X_{\zeta}
, i.e.
\underset{p+q=\bullet}{{\mathrm{Tot}}}\left(E^{p,q}_{\infty}\right)={\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta})
. Here
\mathcal{U}
is a good cover of
S
. Moreover, the second page of this spectral sequence can be identified with

\displaystyle(\check{{\mathrm{H}}}^{\bullet_{1}}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}\mathcal{U},{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet_{2}}(X_{\zeta}/S)),{\mathrm{d}}^{p,q}_{2}).

Proposition 7.5
.

Lets assume that
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SU}}(m/2)
and let
X_{\zeta}\rightarrow S
be an ACF fibration whose fibres are ALE spaces resolving

. There exists an isomorphism

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{2}\cong\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}{\mathrm{H}}^{p}(S)&\text{if }q=0\\
{\mathrm{H}}^{p}(S,\mathcal{H}^{q}(X_{\zeta}/S))&\text{else}\end{array}\right.

i.e.

\displaystyle E^{\bullet,\bullet}_{2}\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S)\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(L^{2}GM^{\bullet}(\nu_{\zeta}:X_{\zeta}\rightarrow S)).

Moreover, for
0<N
the differentials
d_{2N}=0
.

.

Assume
(X_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta},g_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta})
