7.3.
Fredholm Theory for Adiabatic Families of Asymptotically Conically Fibred Dirac Operators

In the following we will use the adiabatic tools established in section
7.2.3
to study the analytic realisation of the family of ACF Dirac operators
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
. We will construct certain “adapted adiabatic norms” and prove uniform elliptic estimates.

In the following we will construct adapted norms that allow uniform elliptic estimates. These norms will be essential in the uniform linear gluing results needed for the applications mentioned in the preface.

Definition 7.9
.

We define the
C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}
-pushforeword of the vector bundle
E_{\zeta}
to be the infinite dimensional vector bundle

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(U,(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{AC;\beta}^{k}}E_{\zeta})

\displaystyle:=C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}(\nu^{-1}_{\zeta}(U),E_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}})

for all
U\subset S
open. The fibres of these vector bundle are given by the vector spaces

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s),E_{\zeta(s)}).

We will write

\displaystyle(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{AC;\beta}^{k,\alpha}}\pi_{\zeta}:(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{AC;\beta}^{k,\alpha}}E_{\zeta}\rightarrow S.

We will denote the
l\leq k
norms on
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{AC;\beta}^{k,\alpha}}E_{\zeta}
respectively by

\displaystyle\left|\psi\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}(s)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\psi(s)\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}(\nu^{-1}_{\zeta}(s),E_{\zeta(s)})}.

Definition 7.10
.

We define the Hölder space of sections of
\nu_{*}E_{\zeta}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\psi\right|\right|_{C^{(k,l),\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\underset{S}{{\mathrm{sup}}}\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes_{\zeta};H})^{i}\psi\right|_{C^{l-i,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle+\underset{\underset{{\mathrm{dist}}_{g_{S}}(s,s^{\prime})<{\mathrm{inj}}_{g_{S}}}{s,s^{\prime}\in S,}}{{\mathrm{sup}}}\left\{\frac{\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes_{\zeta};H})^{i}\psi(s)-\Pi^{s,s^{\prime}}(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes_{\zeta};H})^{i}\psi(y)\right|_{C^{l-i,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}}{d_{g_{S}}(s,s^{\prime})^{\alpha}}\right\}

and define

\displaystyle\left|\left|\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\psi\right|\right|_{C^{(k,k),\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}.

Notice, that by definition the Banach spaces

(67)

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}})\cong

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\oplus\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

(68)

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}})\cong

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{I}^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\oplus\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta-1}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

Assumption 5
.

In the following we will assume that
E_{\zeta}=(E_{\zeta})_{-q}
, i.e. if
\psi\in(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{C^{k}_{loc}}}(E_{\zeta})_{-q}

\displaystyle\left|\psi\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}=t^{-q-\beta}\cdot\left|\psi\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;1}}.

in order to keep the following proves readable and calculations short.

Lemma 7.12
.

Let
\Phi\in C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}})
and let
X\in\mathfrak{X}^{1,0}(X_{\zeta})
and
U\in\mathfrak{X}^{0,1}(X_{\zeta})
of unite norm with respect to
g^{t}_{\zeta}
. Then there exists a
\phi\geq 1

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left[\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}_{X},\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}_{U}\right]\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}\lesssim t^{\phi}\cdot\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

Proof.

This follows from (
10
) and the definition of the ACF Hölder norms.
∎

Lemma 7.13
.

The identifications

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}})\cong

\displaystyle C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{0}_{\zeta}})\cong

\displaystyle C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{I}^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta-1}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

are isomorphisms of Banach spaces.

Proof.

A moment of thought reveals that the only “off-diagonal” terms are dependent on the commutator estimate

\displaystyle[\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta};H},D_{\zeta;V}].

∎

Remark 56
.

Notice that the bundle
\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\subset(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta-1}}E_{\zeta}
and hence there exists an embedding

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\hookrightarrow(\nu_{\zeta})_{*^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}.

\heartsuit

Definition 7.11
.

We define the identifications

\displaystyle\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}:C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

\displaystyle\cong C^{k,\alpha}_{t}(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})):\iota_{\mathcal{K};\beta}

\displaystyle\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}:C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

\displaystyle\cong C^{k,\alpha}_{t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})):\iota_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}

Lemma 7.14
.

Let
\beta>-m/2
and
\beta+\mu_{\mathcal{K}}<\gamma
. The following for the operator norms of
\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}
and
\iota_{\mathcal{K};\beta}
. Let
\psi\in\Gamma^{k,\alpha}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)
and
\Phi\in\Gamma^{k,\alpha}_{loc}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})
and let
\beta+\mu_{\mathcal{K}}<\gamma
. Then

(69)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\iota_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-\beta-m/2}\cdot\left|\left|\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

(70)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2+\beta-\alpha}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{(k,0),\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

(71)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\iota_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{1-\beta-m/2}\cdot\left|\left|\psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

(72)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2+\beta-1-\alpha}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{(k,0),\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}.

Proof.

The proof of the first two bounds are analogous to the second two. Thus, we will resume by proofing the
t
-dependence of the operator norms of
\iota_{\mathcal{K};\beta}
and
\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}
.

If
k-m/2\geq k+\alpha\geq k-m/p
,
-m/2<\beta
when ever
2>p
or
-m/2\leq\beta
, and
\delta>\beta
, then by the weighted embedding theorems for AC-spaces
[
Mar02
]
there exists a continuous embeddings

\displaystyle H^{k}_{AC;\beta}\hookrightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}\hookrightarrow W^{k,p}_{AC;\delta}.

Therefore we can bound the norms

\displaystyle\left|\psi\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\psi\right|_{H^{k}_{AC;\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\psi\right|_{L^{2}_{AC;\beta;t}}\lesssim t^{m/2+\beta}\cdot\left|\psi\right|_{L^{2}_{AC;t}}.

Let
e^{1}_{k}
be an orthonormal basis of
(\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})_{s})_{-q}
with respect to
L^{2}_{1}
. Then

\displaystyle 1=

\displaystyle\left|\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{t}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M_{\zeta(s)}}\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|^{2}_{h^{t}_{\zeta(s)}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{t^{2}g_{\zeta}(s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{m-2q}\int_{M_{\zeta(s)}}\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|^{2}_{h_{\zeta(s)}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g_{\zeta}(s)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t^{m-2q}\left|\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{1}}^{2}.

Let
e^{t}_{n}=t^{q-m/2}e^{1}_{k}
be a local, orthogonal, parallel frame of
\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
. Then we can bound

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}})^{i}\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta}}(s)\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{n}\left|\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left<(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{i}\Phi(s),e^{t}_{n}\right>_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left|\left<(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{i}\Phi(s),e^{t}_{n}\right>_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}\right|{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{k}\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}w_{ACF;\beta;t}\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{i}\Phi(s)\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}

\displaystyle\cdot w_{ACF;-\beta;t}\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2+\beta}\left|\left|\Phi(s)\right|\right|_{C^{(l,0)}_{ACF;\beta;t}}.

Let
s,s^{\prime}\in S
and
x_{s},x_{s^{\prime}}\in X_{\zeta}
a horizontal pair, i.e.
x_{s^{\prime}}
is the end point of every horizontal path lifting a path connecting
s
and
s^{\prime}
. In particular
{\mathrm{dist}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}(x_{s},x_{s^{\prime}})={\mathrm{dist}}_{g_{S}}(s,s^{\prime})
and

\displaystyle\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi(s)-\Pi^{\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}}}_{s,s^{\prime}}\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi(s^{\prime})\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left<\Phi(x_{s})-\Pi^{\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}}}_{x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime}}\Phi(x_{s}^{\prime}),e^{t}_{n}\right>_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left|\Phi(x_{s})-\Pi^{\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}}}_{x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime}}\Phi(x_{s}^{\prime})\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}\cdot\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}w_{ACF;\beta-\alpha;t}(x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime})\frac{\left|\Phi(x_{s})-\Pi^{\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}}}_{x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime}}\Phi(x_{s}^{\prime})\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}}{{\mathrm{dist}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}(x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime})^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\cdot w_{ACF;\alpha-\beta;t}(x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime})\cdot{\mathrm{dist}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}(x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime})^{\alpha}\cdot\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left[\Phi(s)\right]_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}w_{ACF;\alpha-\beta;t}(x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime})\cdot{\mathrm{dist}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}(x_{s},x_{s}^{\prime})^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\cdot\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|_{h^{t}_{\zeta},g^{t}_{\zeta};V}{\mathrm{vol}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta};V}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2+\beta-\alpha}\cdot\left[\Phi(s)\right]_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}{\mathrm{dist}}_{g_{S}}(s,s^{\prime})^{\alpha}

which concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.15
.

The fibrewise asymptotically conical Schauder estimates

(73)

\displaystyle\left|\Phi\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|t^{-1}\cdot D_{\zeta;V}\Phi\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\Phi\right|_{C^{0}_{AC;\beta;t}}

hold. By running a contradiction argument as in
[
Wal17a
, Prop. 8.7]
we can improve on it by

(74)

\displaystyle\left|\Phi\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|t^{-1}\cdot D_{\zeta;V}\Phi\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta-1;t}}\lesssim\left|\Phi\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}

where (
74
) holds for all
\Phi\in C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}^{k,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))
.

In the following we will improve these Schauder estimate, by bounding individual components of

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{II}}&\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{KI}}\\
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}&\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\\
\end{array}\right)

As mentioned previously, the horizontal Dirac operator restricts to a differential operator on
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta}}E_{\zeta}
and
(\nu_{\zeta})_{*_{ACF;\beta-1}}E_{\zeta}
. In order to improve the Schauder estimate for the components of
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta}
we need to bound the “naive” off-diagonal elements.

Lemma 7.16
.

Let

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{K}}\geq\phi-m/2-\beta

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\iota_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\geq\phi-m/2-\beta

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{I}}\geq\phi+m/2+\beta-1-\alpha

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}}\geq\phi+m/2+\beta-1-\alpha

Then the following estimates hold true

(75)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\iota_{\mathcal{K}}}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

(76)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{K}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

(77)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{KI}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\iota_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

(78)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\pi_{\mathcal{K}}}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

Proof.

Using the (
60
) and (
61
) we bound

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|R_{V;\beta}\{D_{\zeta;V},\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\iota_{\mathcal{K}}}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{K}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\{D_{\zeta;V},\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\}L_{V;-\beta-m-1}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}}}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{KI}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|L_{V;-\beta-m-1}\{D_{\zeta;V},\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\iota_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H;\beta;\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\{D_{\zeta;V},\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H}\}R_{V;\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\pi_{\mathcal{I}}}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

∎

Using the above, we can improve the ACF Schauder estimate (
7.1
) on the components of
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
.

Let in the following

\displaystyle\iota\geq

\displaystyle\phi-m/2-\beta\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\pi\geq\phi+m/2+\beta-1-\alpha

Proposition 7.6

(Improved ACF Schauder Estimates)
.

The following estimates hold

(79)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{I}}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

(80)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{KI}}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\iota}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

(81)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\pi}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

The map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))\cap C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{t}(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))

satisfies

(82)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}+\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{t}},

is Fredholm, and its kernel and cokernel coincide with

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ker}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}})={\mathrm{ker}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{K}})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt{\mathrm{coker}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}})={\mathrm{coker}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}})

Remark 57
.

Notice that if
(X_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})=(N\times M_{\zeta},g_{N}\times g_{\zeta;V})/H\rightarrow(N,g_{N})/H=(S,g_{S})
for some finite subgroup
H\subset{\mathrm{SO}}(n)
then both

\displaystyle\pi=\iota=\infty.

This will be relevant in constructing examples of compact torsion free
{\mathrm{Spin}}(7)
-manifolds, as most resolutions of orbifold singularities will be of this form.

\heartsuit

Remark 58
.

In order to prove (
79
) we will follow the proof of
[
Wal17a
, Prop. 8.3]
and it suffices to prove the statement for
k=0
.

\heartsuit

Proof of (
79
).

Following the above proof we only need to prove that

(83)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;{\mathcal{II}}}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}.

holds. Again assume that (
83
) is false and that there exists a sequence
(t_{i},\Phi_{i},x_{i})_{i}
such that

\displaystyle t_{i}\to 0\hskip 28.45274pt\left|\left|\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t_{i}}}\leq C\hskip 28.45274ptw^{t_{i}}_{\zeta;\beta}|\Phi_{i}|_{h^{t_{i}}_{\zeta}}(x_{i})=1

\displaystyle\left|\left|(D^{\otimes^{t_{i}}_{\zeta}}_{H;\mathcal{II}}+t_{i}^{-1}\cdot D_{\zeta;V})\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t_{i}}}\to 0.

In contrast to the previous case we need to distinguish between two cases.

Case 1
: The sequence
x_{i}
has a bounded subsequence and consequently by passing through a subsequence we can assume that
x_{i}
converges in the exceptional set
\Upsilon_{\zeta}
. We define a new sequence by

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\hat{\Phi}_{i}=(t_{i})^{q+\beta}\pi_{\mathcal{I}}\Phi_{i}.

By using Arzelà-Ascoli and after passing through another subsequence we can extract a limit

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\hat{\Phi}_{\infty}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;1}}\leq C\hskip 28.45274pt\left|\left|D_{\zeta;V}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\hat{\Phi}_{\infty}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;1}}=0.

As
D_{\zeta;V}
is injective on sections on
\mathcal{I}^{1,\alpha}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})
by (
74
) but

\displaystyle\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\hat{\Phi}_{\infty}\right|_{h^{1}_{\zeta}}(\hat{x}_{\infty})=1

and hence we arrived at a contradiction.

Case 2
: The sequence
x_{i}\to\infty
, i.e. it leaves every compact subset of
X_{\zeta}
. We know that
x_{i}\to\infty

\displaystyle(E_{\zeta},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}},h^{t}_{\zeta},\hat{\nabla}^{\otimes^{t}_{\zeta}})(x_{i})\to(E_{0},{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{0}},h^{t}_{0},\nabla^{\otimes^{t}_{0}})(\rho_{\zeta}(x_{i}))

uniformally. Hence we define a new sequence by

\displaystyle\hat{\Phi}_{i}=(t_{i}r(x_{i}))^{q+\beta}\Psi^{*}_{r(x_{i})}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi_{i}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\hat{x}_{i}=\frac{\rho_{\zeta}(x_{i})}{r(\rho_{\zeta}(x_{i}))}.

on
X_{\zeta}\backslash E_{\zeta}\cong X_{0}\backslash S
. By using Arzelà-Ascoli and after passing through another subsequence we can extract a limit

\displaystyle\left|\left|\hat{\Phi}_{\infty}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;1}}\leq C\hskip 28.45274pt\left|\left|D_{0;V}\hat{\Phi}_{\infty}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1;1}}=0.

The operator
D_{0;V}
is injective and

\displaystyle\left|\hat{\Phi}_{\infty}\right|_{h^{1}_{\zeta}}(\hat{x}_{\infty})=1.

Hence we arrived at a contradiction.
∎

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\{\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;H},D_{\zeta;V}\}L_{V;-\beta-m-1}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

Proof of (
82
).

Combining (
82
) and (
80
) we deduce that (
76
)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}+\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta;\beta;\mathcal{K}\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}}\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}+\underbrace{\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}}_{\subset\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}}_{\subset\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}}+\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{t}}.

∎

Definition 7.12
.

Let
-\iota<\kappa<\pi
. We define the adiabatic norms adapted to the adiabatic family of Dirac operators
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
to be

(84)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+t^{-\kappa}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

(85)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+t^{-\kappa}\cdot\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}.

Definition 7.13
.

We define the space

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}):=\left({\mathrm{dom}}_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}),\left|\left|.\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}\right)

equipped with the graph norm

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}=\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}.

Corollary 7.2
.

The space
\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})
decomposes into

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})={\mathrm{coim}}_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus\mathfrak{Ker}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}).

Proof.

The space
C^{k+1,\alpha}(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))
splits into
{\mathrm{coim}}_{C^{k+1,\alpha}}(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\oplus{\mathrm{ker}}((\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta})
. By Lemma
7.13
we conclude the statement.
∎

Proposition 7.7
.

The space
\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})
is a Banach space and the map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}

is Fredholm. The kernel and cokernel are smooth, i.e. independent of
\alpha
and
k
. Moreover,

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})=\mathfrak{ind}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})

and hence if
\beta_{2}<\beta_{1}
is a critical rate, then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{2}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})-{\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta_{1}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})=\sum_{\beta_{2}<\lambda<\beta_{1}}d_{\lambda+\frac{m-1}{2}-\delta(E_{0})}(\widehat{D}_{0})

Proof.

Again this statement follows immediately from Proposition
86
, elliptic bootstrapping and the proof of Proposition
6.3
.
∎

Proposition 7.8
.

Assume that
-\iota<\kappa<\pi
. Then the operator

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}

satisfies

(86)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+t^{-\kappa}\cdot\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{t}}

Further, let
\Phi\in\mathfrak{Ker}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})^{\perp}
. Then

(87)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}.

Proof of Proposition
7.8
.

We can bound the first term by

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{I};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\underbrace{t^{\iota}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}}_{\subset t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}}

\displaystyle t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}+t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}+\underbrace{t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{t}}}_{\subset\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}}+t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{t}}

where the second term can also be absorb into left hand side of (
87
). The remanding terms sum up to the
\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}
which completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

If
D^{t}
is isentropic then it admits a uniformally bounded right-inverse

\displaystyle R_{D^{t}}:\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X^{t},E^{t})\rightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X^{t},E^{t}).

8.
Uniform Elliptic Theory for Dirac Operators on Orbifold Resolutions of Type (I)

In the following section we will proceed with constructing the uniform elliptic theory for the Dirac operator
D^{t}
on
X^{t}
, which will be derived by combining our understanding of the adiabatic limits of families of Dirac operators on
X_{\zeta}
and the elliptic theory of Dirac operators on orbifolds developed in section
6.0.3
.

8.1.
Weighted Function Spaces on Resolutions of Orbifolds

In the following we will introduce a family of weighted Banach spaces of sections of the Dirac bundle on the orbifold resolution. As the orbifold resolution is assumed to be a compact manifold, these weights will only contribute to the operator norms and not the Fredholm properties of the analytic realisations. The upshot of choosing these weights is that they can be used to proof uniform operator bounds which are necessary for proving gluing theorems on the orbifold resolution. In order to prove these statements we rely on the fact that the subject norm and the ACF norms are equivalent for sections supported close to the exceptional set.

Notation
.

In the following we will need to vary the width of the tubular neighbourhood of the singular stratum. Hence we will set

\displaystyle\epsilon\sim t^{\lambda}

for a
1>\lambda\geq 0
.

5
\epsilon^{-1}r
\chi_{1}
\chi_{2}
\chi_{3}
\chi_{4}
\chi_{5}
