Plot of
\chi_{i}
and their complements.

Definition 8.1
.

We define the function spaces

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X^{t},E^{t})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\overline{\mathfrak{D}}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(\overline{D}^{t},+)

by completing
\Gamma^{k,\alpha}(X^{t},E^{t})
and
\Gamma^{k,\alpha}_{c}(\overline{X}^{t},\overline{E}^{t})
with respect to the norms

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}=\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t}\left((1-\chi_{4})\cdot\Phi\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\chi_{2}\cdot\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(\overline{D}^{t})}=\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t}\left(\chi_{2}\cdot\Phi\right)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}+\left|\left|(1-\chi_{4})\cdot\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}.

Definition 8.2
.

The projection

\displaystyle\cup^{t}:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X^{t},E^{t}),\,(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\mapsto(\Psi\cup^{t}\Phi)

has a section

\displaystyle\cap^{t}:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X^{t},E^{t})\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+),\,\Psi\mapsto\left(\delta^{*}_{t}(1-\chi_{4})\Phi,\chi_{2}\Phi\right).

as well as the projection
\overline{\cup}^{t}
has a section
\overline{\cap}^{t}
defined in an analogous way.

Lemma 8.1
.

The Banach spaces
\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(D^{t})
and
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(X^{t},E^{t})
are homeomorphic; the Banach spaces
\overline{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(\overline{D}^{t})
and the Banach spaces
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}
are homeomorphic. Moreover,

\displaystyle\min\left\{1,t^{\lambda(k+\alpha-\beta)},t^{\kappa-m/2}\right\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(D^{t})}\lesssim\max\left\{1,t^{-\lambda\beta},t^{-\kappa+m/2}\right\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}}=\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(\overline{D}^{t})}\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}

holds. The analogous statements hold for
\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X^{t},E^{t})
and
C^{k,\alpha}_{t}(X^{t},E^{t})
, as well as
\overline{\mathfrak{C}}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(\overline{X}^{t},\overline{E}^{t})
and
C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1;}(X_{0},E_{0})
. Moreover, given a section
\Phi\in\Gamma^{k+1,\alpha}_{loc}(X^{t},E^{t})
with support in
A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S)
, then

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(D^{t})}\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF-1;\beta}}

Proof.

By the definition of the weighted CSF-norms, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\chi_{2}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}\lesssim\max\{1,t^{-\lambda\beta}\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(X^{t}\backslash U^{t}_{\epsilon})}

and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(X^{t}\backslash U^{t}_{2\epsilon})}\lesssim\max\{1,t^{\lambda(\beta-k-\alpha)}\}\left|\left|\chi_{2}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}.

Further, using the definition of the adiabatic ACF norms, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t}(1-\chi_{4})\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim\max\left\{1,t^{-\lambda\beta},t^{-\kappa+m/2}\right\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(U^{t}_{4\epsilon})}

and

\displaystyle\min\left\{1,t^{\lambda(k+\alpha-\beta)},t^{\kappa-m/2}\right\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(U^{t}_{3\epsilon})}\lesssim\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t}(1-\chi_{4})\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}.

we use

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;0;t}}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(U^{t}_{4\epsilon})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(U^{t}_{4\epsilon})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{1,t^{-\lambda\beta}\right\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(U^{t}_{4\epsilon})}

\displaystyle t^{-\kappa}\left|\left|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\kappa+m/2}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}(U^{t}_{4\epsilon})}

In order to show the uniform equivalence of the norms on the gluing neck, we observe that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{I};\beta}\chi^{t}_{2}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{2}\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;0;t}}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash U^{t}_{\epsilon})}\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash U^{t}_{\epsilon})}

and since

\displaystyle\nabla^{\oplus_{0}}\chi_{i}=\epsilon^{-1}\partial_{r}\chi_{i}{\mathrm{d}}r

we deduce that

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{i}\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\chi^{t}_{2}\Phi\right|_{C^{k-i}_{AC;\beta;t}}(s)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left|\left<(\hat{\nabla}^{g^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{j}({\chi^{t}_{2}})\wedge(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{i-j}\Phi(s),e^{t}_{n}\right>_{h^{t}_{\zeta;V}}\right|{\mathrm{vol}}_{t^{2}g_{\zeta;V}}\left|e^{t}_{n}\right|_{C^{k-i,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-m/2-\beta}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{i}\sum_{n}\int_{\nu_{\zeta}^{-1}(s)}\left|\left<(\hat{\nabla}^{g^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{j}({\chi^{t}_{2}})\wedge(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta};H})^{i-j}\Phi(s),e^{t}_{n}\right>_{h^{t}_{\zeta;V}}\right|{\mathrm{vol}}_{t^{2}g_{\zeta;V}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{-m/2-\beta}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{i}\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}})^{i-j}\Phi(s)\right|_{C^{0}_{AC;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash B_{t^{-1}\epsilon}(X_{\zeta}))}\int^{\infty}_{t^{\lambda-1}}(rt)^{\beta}t^{j(1-\lambda)}r^{1-m}r^{\mu_{\mathcal{K}}}t^{m}r^{m-1}{\mathrm{d}}r

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2+(\lambda-1)(\beta+\mu_{\mathcal{K}}+1)}\left|(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}})^{i}\Phi(s)\right|_{C^{k-i,\alpha}_{AC;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash B_{t^{-1}\epsilon}(X_{\zeta}))}.

A moment of thought reveals the additional exponent
(\lambda-1)\alpha
in the presence of the Hölder exponent. Hence we can deduce

\displaystyle\left|\left|\pi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\chi^{t}_{2}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{m/2+(\lambda-1)(\beta+\mu_{\mathcal{K}}+1+\alpha)}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash U^{t}_{\epsilon})}.

The other estimates follow from the definition of the norms and are left to the involved reader.
∎

Definition 8.3
.

We define the approximate kernel and cokernel of
D^{t}
by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})=\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathrm{ker}}\left(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\right)\\
\oplus\\
{\mathrm{ker}}\left(D:C^{\bullet,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\rightarrow C^{\bullet-1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}(X,E)\right)\end{array}

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})=\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathrm{coker}}\left(\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K};\beta}:C^{k+1,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\rightarrow C^{k,\alpha}_{t}\left(S,\mathcal{C}o\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta})\right)\right)\\
\oplus\\
{\mathrm{coker}}\left(D:C^{\bullet,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\rightarrow C^{\bullet-1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}(X,E)\right).\end{array}

Example 9
.

Let
D={\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}^{*}
and
(X_{\zeta},g_{\zeta})
be given by a fibration of ALE spaces. Then the approximate kernel for large enough
\beta<-m/2
is isomorphic

\displaystyle{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})\cong{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(S,\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}(X_{\zeta}/S))\oplus{\mathrm{H}}^{\bullet}(X).

\clubsuit

8.2.
Uniform Linear Gluing for Dirac Operators on Orbifold Resolutions

In section
4.1
we established that the equation

\displaystyle D^{t}(\Psi\cup^{t}\Phi)=0.

can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\cup^{t}\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)=0

with

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi):=\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Psi+(D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})(1-\chi^{t}_{5})\Psi+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3})(\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi-\Psi)

and

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi):=D\Phi+(D_{t}-D)\chi_{1}\Phi+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})(\Phi-\delta_{t^{-1}}^{*}\Psi)

Using the uniform Fredholm theories for
D
and
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
developed in section
6
and
7
we are able to prove the following statement.

Theorem 8.1

(Uniform Linear Gluing)
.

The constants
\alpha
,
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
,
\lambda
,
\kappa
can be chosen such that

\displaystyle-\phi+m/2+\beta<\kappa<\phi+m/2+\beta-1-\alpha.

The operator
D^{t}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

for all
\Phi\in{\mathrm{xker}}(D^{t})^{\perp}
. Moreover, there exists an exact sequence

(88)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{t})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{coker}}(D^{t})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{t}_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{t}_{\beta}}

which implies the existence of a uniformally bounded right-inverse of
D^{t}
if
{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}=0
.

Remark 59
.

By taking the Euler characteristic of the sequence, we deduce that
{\mathrm{ind}}\circ\cup^{t}=+\circ\mathfrak{ind}
, i.e.

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ind}}(D^{t})={\mathrm{ind}}_{\beta}(D)+\mathfrak{ind}_{\beta}(\widehat{D}^{0}_{\zeta}).

\heartsuit

Remark 60
.

Notice that (
88
) is an exact sequence of finite dimensional vector spaces. If

\displaystyle{\mathrm{dim}}({\mathrm{ker}}(D^{t}))={\mathrm{dim}}({\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t}))

for some
\beta\notin\mathcal{C}(\widehat{D}_{0})
then
{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}=0
.

\heartsuit

8.2.1.
Proof of Theorem
8.1

The following section is devoted to the proof of the Linear-Gluing-Theorem
8.1
for Dirac operators on orbifold resolutions.

Before we start we need to compare the operator
D^{t}
with
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
and
D
. These are bounds on the non-leading order terms of
\Theta^{t}_{ACF}
and
\Theta^{t}_{ACF}
. In order to prove these estimates we will begin with the following technical lemmas.

Proposition 8.1
.

The commutator estimates

(89)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\cap^{t}\circ D^{t}-\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D\right)\circ\cap^{t}\right)\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}

(90)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(D^{t}\circ\cup^{t}-\cup^{t}\circ\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D\right)\right)(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}

(91)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overline{\cap}^{t}\circ\overline{D}^{t}-\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D\right)\circ\overline{\cap}^{t}\right)\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(\overline{D}^{t})}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}

(92)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overline{D}^{t}\circ\overline{\cup}^{t}-\overline{\cup}^{t}\circ\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D\right)\right)(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\overline{\mathfrak{C}}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}

hold.

Proof.

In order to proof (
89
) we observe that

\displaystyle LHS=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left((1-\chi^{t}_{3})D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\right)\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\left(\chi_{3}D_{t}-D\chi_{3}\right)\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\underbrace{\left|\left|(D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}}_{(i)}+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3})\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}}_{\lesssim\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon}(S))}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|(D_{t}-D)\chi_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{(ii)}+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}

where by
(i)
and
(ii)
can be bounded by

\displaystyle(i)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|(D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}})\circ\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}\circ(\hat{\nabla}^{h^{t}_{\zeta}}-\nabla^{h^{t}})\right)(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{\lambda}\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle(ii)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|(D_{t}-D)\chi_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(({\mathrm{cl}}_{g}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}})\circ\hat{\nabla}^{h}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}\circ(\hat{\nabla}^{h}-\nabla^{h_{t}})\right)\chi_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)}\left|\left|\chi_{2}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}.

In a similar manner we bound (
90
) by

\displaystyle LHS=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(D_{t}-\widehat{D}_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}\right)(1-\chi_{3})\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi+\left(D_{t}-D\right)\chi_{3}\Phi+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\left(D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\right)(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\chi_{2}\left(D_{t}-D\right)\chi_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\chi_{2}\left(D_{t}-\widehat{D}_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}\right)(1-\chi_{3})\delta_{t^{-1}}^{*}\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\left(D^{t}-\delta_{t}^{*}D\right)\chi^{t}_{3}\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3})-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3}))\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|({\mathrm{cl}}_{g}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3}))\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{\lesssim\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})(\Psi-\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})(\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi)\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{\lesssim\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}.

Again, by a similar argument we bound (
91
) by

\displaystyle LHS=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\chi^{t}_{3}\overline{D}^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\chi^{t}_{3}\right)\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}+\left|\left|\left((1-\chi_{3})\overline{D}_{t}-D(1-\chi_{3})\right)\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{0})\chi^{t}_{3}\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}+\left|\left|(D-\widehat{D}_{0})(1-\chi_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{0}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3})\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})}+\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{\lesssim\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\overline{\mathfrak{D}}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(\overline{D}^{t})}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{2\epsilon,3\epsilon}(S))}.

Lastly, (
92
) is bounded by

\displaystyle LHS=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left(\overline{D}_{t}-D\right)(1-\chi_{3})\Psi+\left(\overline{D}_{t}-\widehat{D}_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}\right)\chi_{3}\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Phi+{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Phi-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{2}\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\right)\chi^{t}_{3}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|(1-\chi_{4})\left(\overline{D}_{t}-D\right)(1-\chi_{3})\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|(1-\chi_{4})\left(\widehat{D}_{0}-\widehat{D}_{t^{2}\cdot\zeta}\right)\chi_{3}\delta_{t^{-1}}^{*}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\chi^{t}_{2}\left(\overline{D}^{t}-\delta^{t}_{*}D\right)(1-\chi^{t}_{3})\delta^{*}_{t}\Psi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{\zeta}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3})-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{0}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi^{t}_{3}))\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|({\mathrm{cl}}_{g}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3}))\Psi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}}}_{\lesssim\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}_{0}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})(\Psi-\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})(\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi)\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{\lesssim\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{\epsilon,2\epsilon}(S))}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\cdot\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}+\left|\left|\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\Psi-\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}(A_{\epsilon,2\epsilon}(S))}

∎

Proposition 8.2
.

The map

(93)

\displaystyle D^{t}:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X^{t},E^{t})\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}(X^{t},E^{t})

is Fredholm and its kernel and cokernel do neither depend on
k
,
\alpha
, nor
\beta
. Furthermore, it satisfies

(94)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0}_{\beta;t}}

which can be improved to

(95)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}

for all
\Phi\in{\mathrm{xker}}(D^{t})^{\perp}
. Consequently the projections

\displaystyle\Pi_{L^{2}_{t}}:{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})\twoheadrightarrow{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{t})\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\Pi_{L^{2}_{t}}:{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})\twoheadrightarrow{\mathrm{coker}}(D^{t})

are surjective.

Proof.

Notice, that the
\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}
/
\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}
-norms are
t
-dependent equivalent to the Hölder spaces of sections of
E^{t}\rightarrow X^{t}
. Hence, the first statement follows from standard elliptic regularity of
D^{t}
.

By Proposition
6.2
and Proposition
7.1
we conclude

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}=

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\cdot\delta^{*}_{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\chi_{2}\cdot\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}((1-\chi^{t}_{4})\cdot\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1,t}}+\left|\left|D(\chi_{2}\cdot\Phi)\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1,t}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\cdot\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0}_{ACF;\beta;t}}+\left|\left|\chi_{2}\cdot\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CSF;\beta;t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t}}+\underbrace{\left|\left|\left(\cap^{t}\circ D^{t}-\left(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D\right)\circ\cap^{t}\right)\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}}_{\left\{\subset\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\right\}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(t^{-1}\epsilon,2t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0}_{\beta;t}}

In order to show the uniform Schauder estimate

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t}}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0}_{\beta;t}}

it remains to prove that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(t^{-1}\epsilon,2t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}\lesssim\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t}}.

By Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
7.3
we know that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(t^{-1}\epsilon,2t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}+\underbrace{\left|\left|(D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{0})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}}_{(i)}

\displaystyle+\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{0}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}.

and as
(i)
can be bounded by

\displaystyle(i)=

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}\circ(\nabla^{h^{t}}-\nabla^{\otimes_{0}})+({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}})\circ\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}\right)\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left|\left|{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}\circ(\nabla^{h^{t}}-\nabla^{\otimes_{0}})\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}+\left|\left|({\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}-{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{0}})\circ\nabla^{\otimes_{0}}\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(A_{(2t^{-1}\epsilon,3t^{-1}\epsilon)}(S))}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\left\{t^{(\lambda-1)(\eta+1)},t^{\lambda}\right\}\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}}

it can be absorbed into the left-hand side of the Schauder estimate (
94
).

We will now proceed with proving the uniform lower boundedness of
D^{t}
on
{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}
. By using (
94
) it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0}_{\beta;t}}\lesssim\left|\left|D^{t}\Phi\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1,t}}.

We will prove this estimate by contradiction. Assume that (
95
) is false and that there exist sequences
t_{i}\to 0
and
\Phi_{i}\perp{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})
such that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0}_{\beta;t_{i}}}=1,\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\left|\left|D^{t^{i}}\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1;t_{i}}}\to 0

Using the uniform Schauder estimate we know that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{1,\alpha}_{\beta;t_{i}}}\leq 1

is bounded and by passing to a subsequence we can arrange that

\displaystyle\left|\left|(1-\chi^{t}_{4})\delta_{t}^{*}\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{0,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t_{i}}}+\left|\left|\chi_{2}\Phi_{i}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon_{i}}}\lesssim C

Further, we pick a sequence
x_{i}\in X^{t_{i}}
such that

\displaystyle|w_{\beta;t_{i}}\Phi_{i}|_{h^{t_{i}}}(x_{i})=1.

After passing through a subsequence we can reduce the contradiction argument to one of the following cases:

1.

The sequence
x_{i}
accumulates near the exceptional set of the resolution
\rho^{t}:X^{t}\dashrightarrow X

\Rightarrow
ACF regime

2.

The sequence
x_{i}
accumulates in the gluing neck of the space
X^{t}

\Rightarrow
CF regime

3.

The sequence
x_{i}
accumulates on the regular part of the resolution
\rho^{t}:X^{t}\dashrightarrow X

\Rightarrow
CSF regime

In the following we will show that all three cases will lead to a contradiction.

Case 1:
By passing to a subsequence and by using Arzelà-Ascoli we can extract a limit
x_{\infty}\in X_{\zeta}
and a
\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi_{\infty}\in C^{1,\alpha/2}(S,\mathcal{K}_{AC;\beta}(\pi_{\zeta}/\nu_{\zeta}))
satisfying

\displaystyle 1\lesssim|\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi_{\infty}(\nu_{\zeta}(x_{\infty}))|_{(\nu_{\zeta})_{*}h_{\zeta}}

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\varpi_{\mathcal{K};\beta}\Phi_{\infty}=0

which contradicts
\Phi_{\infty}\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}
.

Case 2:
Assume that the sequence
x_{i}
accumulates in the neck of
\rho^{t_{i}}:X^{t_{i}}\dashrightarrow X
. By using Lemma
6.2
and Lemma
7.3
as well as a rescaling argument and by passing through a subsequence we can apply Arzelà-Ascoli to extract a limit
x_{\infty}\in X_{0}
and
\Phi_{\infty}\in C^{1,\alpha/2}_{CF;\beta}(X_{0},E_{0})
with

\displaystyle 1\lesssim|\Phi_{\infty}(x_{\infty})|_{h_{0}}

and

\displaystyle\widehat{D}_{0}\Phi_{\infty}=0

which is a contradiction to Proposition
5.2
.

Case 3:
By passing through a subsequence and by Arzelà-Ascoli we can
x_{\infty}\in X_{\zeta}
and a
\Phi_{\infty}\in C^{1,\alpha/2}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon_{i}}(X,E)
satisfying

\displaystyle 1\lesssim|\Phi_{\infty}(x_{\infty})|_{h}

and

\displaystyle D\Phi_{\infty}=0

which contradicts
\Phi\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}
.
∎

In the following, we will adapted the discussion of Hutchings and Taubes in
[
HT09
, section 9]
on the linear gluing exact sequence to our setup. We will begin, by proving the existence of solutions to the so-called anti-gluing equation.

Lemma 8.2
.

The so called anti-gluing map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash U^{t}_{2\epsilon},E_{\zeta})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{4\epsilon}(S),E)\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta}\backslash U^{t}_{2\epsilon},E_{\zeta})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{2t^{-1}\epsilon}(Y,E_{Y})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{4\epsilon}(S),E)\\
\oplus\\
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{4\epsilon}(Y,E_{Y})\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\Psi\\
\oplus\\
\Phi\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\\
\oplus\\
{\mathrm{res}}_{2t^{-1}\epsilon}(\Psi)\\
\oplus\\
\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\\
\oplus\\
{\mathrm{res}}_{4\epsilon}(\Phi)\end{array}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\Theta}^{t}}

is an isomorphism of Banach spaces.

Proof.

Notice that following Proposition
5.2
and Lemma
5.3
the map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta;t}(X_{0}\backslash{\mathrm{Tub}}_{2t^{-1}\epsilon},E_{0})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CF;\beta}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{4\epsilon}(S),E_{0})\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1;t}(X_{0}\backslash{\mathrm{Tub}}_{2t^{-1}\epsilon}(S),E_{0})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{2t^{-1}\epsilon}(Y,E_{Y})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k,\alpha}_{CF;\beta-1}({\mathrm{Tub}}_{4\epsilon}(S),E_{0})\\
\oplus\\
C^{k+1,\alpha}_{4\epsilon}(Y,E_{Y})\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\Psi\\
\oplus\\
\Phi\end{array}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{D}^{t}_{0}\Psi\\
\oplus\\
{\mathrm{res}}_{2t^{-1}\epsilon}(\Psi)\\
\oplus\\
\widehat{D}_{0}\\
\oplus\\
{\mathrm{res}}_{4\epsilon}(\Phi)\end{array}}

defines an isomorphism of Banach spaces with a uniformally bounded inverse. Using a left-inverse, we can write the anti-gluing map as (dropping the restriction maps)

\displaystyle\widehat{L}_{0}\Theta^{t}=

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1-\widehat{L}^{t}_{0}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3}^{t})&\widehat{L}^{t}_{0}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g^{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3}^{t})\delta^{*}_{t}\\
\widehat{L}_{0}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}&1+\widehat{L}_{0}{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{t}}({\mathrm{d}}\chi_{3})\delta^{*}_{t^{-1}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\widehat{L}^{t}_{0}\left((D^{t}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})(1-\chi^{t}_{5})+(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}-\widehat{D}^{t}_{0})\right)&0\\
0&\widehat{L}_{0}\left((D_{t}-D)\chi_{1}+(D-\widehat{D}_{0})\right)\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T+S.

Now
T
is invertible with
T^{-1}=2-T
. Using estimate (
92
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\left|S(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{C^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}\lesssim{\mathrm{max}}\left\{t^{\lambda},t^{(1-\lambda)(1+\eta)}\right\}\left|\left|\Psi\oplus\Phi\right|\right|_{C^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}

and we deduce the statement.
∎

Using the existence result for solution of the anti-gluing map we can construct the map
i^{t}_{\beta}
in the following Lemma.

Lemma 8.3
.

Let
\Gamma\in\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(X^{t},E^{t})
,
\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}}\in\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}(X,E)
such that

\displaystyle D^{t}\Gamma=\Xi\cup^{t}{\mathrm{H}}.

Then there exists a unique
\Psi\oplus\Phi\in\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)
such that

\displaystyle\Gamma=\Psi\cup^{t}\Phi\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)=\Xi\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)={\mathrm{H}}.

In particular, the homomorphism

\displaystyle i^{t}_{\beta}:{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{t})\rightarrow{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})

is well-defined.

Proof.

We begin with an arbitrary decomposition of
\Gamma=\Psi^{\prime}\cup^{t}\Phi^{\prime}
. Then

\displaystyle(\Xi-\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi^{\prime}\oplus\Phi^{\prime})\cup^{t}({\mathrm{H}}-\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi^{\prime}\oplus\Phi^{\prime}))=0

and by writing
\Psi\oplus\Phi=(\Psi^{\prime}+\Psi^{\prime\prime})\oplus(\Phi^{\prime}+\Phi^{\prime\prime})
we deduce that

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi^{\prime\prime}\oplus\Phi^{\prime\prime})=

\displaystyle\Xi-\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi^{\prime}\oplus\Phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi^{\prime\prime}\oplus\Phi^{\prime\prime})=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{H}}-\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi^{\prime}\oplus\Phi^{\prime}).

By Lemma
8.2
, this indeed has a solution.
∎

Using Proposition
6.3
and Theorem
7.2
, as well as the ACF- and CSF-wall-crossing formulae we are able to make the following observation.

Proposition 8.3
.

The map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}(X,E)

is Fredholm and its kernel and cokernel does not depend on
k
nor
\alpha
. Moreover, its index does not depend on
\beta
.

Using this we are able to construct the linear obstruction map
{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}
present in the centre of the linear gluing exact sequence.

Lemma 8.4
.

The homomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}:{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})\rightarrow{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})

is well-defined.

Proof.

By Proposition
8.3
the map

\displaystyle\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus D:\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)\rightarrow\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}(X_{\zeta},E_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}(X,E)

satisfies

\displaystyle\left|\left|(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}}\lesssim\left|\left|(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}\Psi,D\Phi)\right|\right|_{\mathfrak{C}^{k,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta-1;t}\oplus C^{k,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta-1;\epsilon}}

on
{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}
. Thus there exists a uniformally bounded left-inverse

\displaystyle\widehat{L}^{t}_{\zeta}\oplus L:{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}\rightarrow{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}

By estimate (
90
) and by a similar argument as in the proof of Lemma
8.2
we deduce that the map

\displaystyle(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\mapsto\left(\widehat{L}^{t}_{\zeta}\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi),L\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)\right)

is invertible. Hence, we deduce that for every
\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}}\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})
there exist unique
\Psi\oplus\Phi\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}

such that

(96)

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Xi+\Psi,H+\Phi)\oplus\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Xi+\Psi,H+\Phi)\in{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t}).

The linear obstruction map is define as

\displaystyle{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}(\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}})=\Theta^{t}_{ACF}((\Xi+\Psi)\oplus(H+\Phi))\oplus\Theta^{t}_{CSF}((\Xi+\Psi)\oplus(H+\Phi)).

∎

Finally, we are able to construct the projection
p^{t}_{\beta}
.

Lemma 8.5
.

The homomorphism

\displaystyle p^{t}_{\beta}:{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})\rightarrow{\mathrm{coker}}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta})

is well defined.

Proof.

This statement follows from the self-adjointness of
D
,
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
and
D^{t}
as well as Proposition
8.2
and by
\beta\to-m-\beta-1
.
∎

Finally, we prove the exactness of the sequence.

Lemma 8.6

(
[
HT09
]
)
.

The sequence (
88
) is exact.

Proof.

We begin by proving the injectivity of
i^{t}_{\beta}
. Consider
\Gamma\in{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{t})
such that
i^{t}_{\beta}(\Gamma)=0
. This implies that
\Gamma=\Psi\cup^{t}\Phi
for
\Psi\oplus\Phi\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}
and
\Theta^{t}_{ACF/CSF}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)=0
. By the uniqueness of the solution to (
96
) with
\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}}=0
we deduce that
\Psi\oplus\Phi=0
which implies
\Gamma=0
. Hence
i^{t}_{\beta}
is injective.

Next, we are going to show that
{\mathrm{ker}}({\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta})={\mathrm{im}}(i^{t}_{\beta})
. By definition
{\mathrm{ker}}({\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta})\supseteq{\mathrm{im}}(i^{t}_{\beta})
and hence it suffice to prove the converse inclusion
{\mathrm{ker}}({\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta})\subseteq{\mathrm{im}}(i^{t}_{\beta})
.

Suppose that
{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)=0
. Then there exists
\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}}\in{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})^{\perp}
such that

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{ACF}((\Xi+\Psi)\oplus({\mathrm{H}}+\Phi))=0\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274pt\Theta^{t}_{ACF}((\Xi+\Psi)\oplus({\mathrm{H}}+\Phi)).

Consequently
\Gamma=(\Xi+\Psi)\cup^{t}({\mathrm{H}}+\Phi)\in{\mathrm{ker}}(D^{t})
and hence
i^{t}_{\beta}(\Gamma)=\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}}
.

Next, we are going to show that
{\mathrm{ker}}(p^{t}_{\beta})={\mathrm{im}}({\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta})
. By the definition of the maps
{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}
and
p^{t}_{\beta}
it suffice to prove that
{\mathrm{im}}({\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta})\supseteq{\mathrm{ker}}(p^{t}_{\beta})
. Let
\Psi\oplus\Phi\in{\mathrm{xcoker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})
and suppose
p^{t}_{\beta}(\Psi\oplus\Phi)=0
. Hence, there exists a
\Gamma\in\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{\beta;t}(D^{t})
such that

\displaystyle D^{t}\Gamma=\Psi\cup^{t}\Phi.

Lemma
8.2
implies the existence of
\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}}\in\mathfrak{D}^{k+1,\alpha}_{ACF;\beta;t}(\widehat{D}^{t}_{0})\oplus C^{k+1,\alpha}_{CSF;\beta;\epsilon}(X,E;+)
such that

\displaystyle\Theta^{t}_{ACF}(\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}})\oplus\Theta^{t}_{CSF}(\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}})=\Psi\oplus\Phi

Then by definition
{\mathrm{ob}}^{t}_{\beta}(\Pi_{{\mathrm{xker}}_{\beta}(D^{t})}(\Xi\oplus{\mathrm{H}})=\Psi\oplus\Phi
.

Lastly, the surjectivity of
p^{t}_{\beta}
follows from Proposition
8.2
.

∎

Appendix A
Riemannian Geometry of Adiabatic Families

The aim of this section is to discuss the decomposition of the Riemannian structures, e.g. Levi-Civita connection, Riemann curvature, etc. on a Riemannian fibration. We will need this deeper understanding in order to study the adiabatic behaviour of Dirac operators. The following discussion is based on the work of Gromoll and Walshap in
[
GW09
]
and Berline, Getzler and Vergne in
[
BGV92
]
.

Let in the following
p:(M,p^{*}g_{S}+g_{V})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
be a Riemannian fibration. In particular, this fibration is equipped with an Ehresmann connection

\displaystyle H=V^{\perp}.

Furthermore, the Riemannian fibration
p:(M,g)\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
carries two canonical connections. The first one, is the Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
with respect to the metric
g
. The second one is the connection

\displaystyle\nabla^{\oplus}=p^{*}\nabla^{g_{S}}\oplus\nabla^{g_{V}}

induced by the lifting the connection
\nabla^{g_{S}}
via the splitting of
TM=H\oplus VX
. Notice that the fibrewise connection
\nabla^{g_{S}}
does not depend on the base metric
g_{S}
.

Let in the following

\displaystyle X,Y,Z\in\mathfrak{X}^{1,0}(M)\hskip 28.45274pt\text{and}\hskip 28.45274ptU,V,W\in\mathfrak{X}^{0,1}(M).

Definition A.1
.

We define the two tensor
A\in\Omega^{1,0}(M,{\mathrm{Hom}}(H,VM))
by

\displaystyle A(X)Y=\nabla^{0,1}_{X}Y=\frac{1}{2}[X,Y]^{0,1}

and

\displaystyle S\in\Omega^{1,0}(M,{\mathrm{End}}(VM))\quad\text{by}\quad S(X)U=-(\nabla_{U}X)^{0,1}.

The second fundamental form of the fibre is given by

\displaystyle{\mathrm{II}}(U,V)=\left<SU,V\right>^{\flat}.

By taking the vertical trace we obtain the fibrewise mean curvature, i.e. the one form

\displaystyle k={\mathrm{tr}}_{g;V}{\mathrm{II}}.

Given
X,Y,Z\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
we define the tensor
\omega\in\Omega^{1}(M,\wedge^{2}T^{*}M)
by

\displaystyle\omega(X)(Y,Z)=

\displaystyle g({\mathrm{II}}(X,Z),Y)-g({\mathrm{II}}(X,Y),Z)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}\left(g(F_{H}(X,Z),Y)-g(F_{H}(X,Y),Z)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.g(F_{H}(Y,Z),X)\right).

Remark 61
.

For a Riemannian fibration, the fibrewise second fundamental form
{\mathrm{II}}
or equivalently
S
vanishes if and only if the fibres are totally geodesic. Furthermore, the tensor
A
vanishes if and only if the Ehresmann connection
H
is flat.

\heartsuit

Remark 62

(
[
BGV92
]
)
.

The de Rham differential on
M
decomposes with respect to
H
and the Riemannian structure
g=p^{*}g_{S}\oplus g_{V}
into

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}^{1,0}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}+{\mathrm{d}}^{2,-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}_{\nabla^{g_{V}}}-\hat{\iota_{{\mathrm{II}}}}+{\mathrm{d}}^{0,1}+\hat{\iota}_{F_{H}}.

In particular, let
{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}
denote the vertical volume form with respect to
g_{V}
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}k(X,Y)=-2{\mathrm{div}}(A(X)Y)

and

\displaystyle{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}=k\wedge{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}+\iota_{F_{H}}{\mathrm{vol}}_{V}.

\heartsuit

Proposition A.1

(
[
BGV92
]
)
.

The Levi-Civita connection
\nabla
and
\nabla^{\oplus}
are related by

\displaystyle g(\nabla_{X}Y,Z)=g(\nabla^{\oplus}_{X}Y,Z)+\omega(X)(Y,Z)

or equivalently

\displaystyle\nabla=\nabla^{\oplus}+\frac{1}{2}\overline{\Lambda}(\omega).

Proposition A.2

(
[
O’N66
,
GW09
]
)
.

Let
R
,
R_{g_{S}}
and
R_{g_{V}}
denote the curvatures of the Levi-Civita connections
\nabla
,
\nabla^{g_{S}}
as well as the fibrewise curvature with respect to
\nabla^{g_{V}}
. The Riemannian curvature tensor of
R
is given by

\displaystyle R^{1,0}(X,Y)Z=

\displaystyle p^{*}R_{g_{S}}(X,Y)Z+2A^{\dagger}(Z)A(X)Y-A^{\dagger}(X)A(Y)Z-A^{\dagger}(Y)A(Z)X

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,Y)Z=

\displaystyle-(\nabla_{Z}A)(X)Y+S(Z)A(X)Y-S(Y)A(Z)X-S(X)A(Y)Z

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,U)Y=

\displaystyle-(\nabla_{U}A)(X)Y-A(Y)A^{\dagger}(X)U-(\nabla_{X}S)(Y)U+S(Y)S(X)U

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,Y)U=

\displaystyle-2(\nabla_{U}A)(X)Y-{\mathrm{d}}^{\nabla}S(X,Y)U+[S(X),S(Y)]U+(A(X)A^{\dagger}(Y)

\displaystyle-A(Y)A^{\dagger}(X))U

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(U,V)W=

\displaystyle R_{g_{V}}(U,V)W-S({\mathrm{II}}(V,W))U+S({\mathrm{II}}(U,W))V

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(U,W)X=

\displaystyle(\nabla_{W}S)(X)U-(\nabla_{U}S)(X)W.

Taking the trace, we deduce that the Ricci curvature of
X
is given by

\displaystyle Ric(X,Y)=

\displaystyle p^{*}Ric_{g_{S}}(X,Y)-\left<A(X),A(Y)\right>-\left<SX,SY\right>+\frac{1}{2}\left(\nabla_{X}k(Y)+\nabla_{Y}k(X)\right)

\displaystyle Ric(U,X)=

\displaystyle\left<\nabla^{0,-1}S(U),X\right>+\nabla_{U}k(X)+\left<\nabla^{-1,0}AX,U\right>-\left<A(X),S(U)\right>

\displaystyle Ric(U,V)=

\displaystyle Ric_{g_{V}}(U,V)-(k,{\mathrm{II}}(U,V))+(AV,AU)+\delta{\mathrm{S}}(U,V)

where

\displaystyle\delta{\mathrm{II}}(U,V)={\mathrm{tr}}\left(X\mapsto(\nabla_{X}{\mathrm{S}})(U,V)\right)

and the scalar curvature is given by

\displaystyle scal=p^{*}scal_{g_{S}}+scal_{g_{F}}-|A|^{2}-|S|^{2}-|k|^{2}-2\nabla^{-1,0}k

where
\nabla^{-1,0}
is the adjoint of
\nabla^{1,0}
with respect to
g
.

Corollary A.1
.

Let
p:(M,g)\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
be a Riemannian fibration with flat, totally geodesic fibres. Then

\displaystyle R^{1,0}(X,Y)Z=

\displaystyle p^{*}R_{g_{S}}(X,Y)Z+2A^{\dagger}(Z)A(X)Y-A^{\dagger}(X)A(Y)Z-A^{\dagger}(Y)A(Z)X

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,Y)Z=

\displaystyle-(\nabla_{Z}A)(X)Y

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,U)Y=

\displaystyle-(\nabla_{U}A)(X)Y-A(Y)A^{\dagger}(X)U

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,Y)U=

\displaystyle-2(\nabla_{U}A)(X)Y+(A(X)A^{\dagger}(Y)-A(Y)A^{\dagger}(X))U

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(U,V)W=

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(U,W)X=

\displaystyle 0.

Taking the trace, we deduce that the Ricci curvature of
X
is given by

\displaystyle Ric(X,Y)=

\displaystyle p^{*}Ric_{g_{S}}(X,Y)-\left<A(X),A(Y)\right>

\displaystyle Ric(U,X)=

\displaystyle\left<\nabla^{-1,0}AX,U\right>

\displaystyle Ric(U,V)=

\displaystyle(AV,AU)

and the scalar curvature is given by

\displaystyle scal=p^{*}scal_{g_{S}}-|A|^{2}=p^{*}scal_{g_{S}}-|F_{H}|^{2}.

Usually, instead of a fixed Riemannian fibration
p:(M,g)\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
we will consider an adiabatic family of Riemannian fibrations, i.e.
p:(M,g^{t}=p^{*}g_{S}+t^{2}g_{V})\rightarrow(S,g_{S})
. In particular, we are interested in the adiabatic limit of this fibrations, i.e. the limit
t\to 0
. The following proposition lists how the Riemannian tensors, e.g. connections and curvatures depend on the parameter
t
.

Proposition A.3

(
[
GW09
]
)
.

By now replacing
g
by
g^{t}
the Levi-Civita connection with respect to
g^{t}
satisfies

\displaystyle\nabla^{g^{t}}_{U}V=

\displaystyle\nabla_{U}V+(t^{2}-1){\mathrm{II}}(U,V)

\displaystyle\nabla^{g^{t}}_{U}X=

\displaystyle\nabla_{U}X+(1-t^{2})A^{\dagger}(X)U

\displaystyle\nabla^{g^{t}}_{X}U=

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}U+(1-t^{2})A^{\dagger}(X)U

\displaystyle\nabla^{g^{t}}_{X}Y=

\displaystyle\nabla_{X}Y.

In particular, its curvature satisfies

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(X,Y)Z=

\displaystyle(1-t^{2})p^{*}R_{g_{S}}(X,Y)Z+t^{2}R^{1,0}(X,Y)Z

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(X,Y)Z=

\displaystyle R_{g}^{0,1}(X,Y)Z

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(U,V)W=

\displaystyle t^{2}(2-t^{2})R^{1,0}_{g}(U,V)W

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(U,V)W=

\displaystyle(1-t^{2})R_{g_{V}}(U,V)W+t^{2}R_{g}^{0,1}(U,V)W

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(X,U)Y=

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,U)Y-(1-t^{2})A(X)A^{\dagger}(Y)U

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(X,U)V=

\displaystyle t^{2}(R^{1,0}(U,X)V-(1-t^{2})A^{\dagger}(A^{\dagger}(V)U

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(U,V)X=

\displaystyle R^{1,0}(U,V)X+(1-t^{2})(A^{\dagger}(X)A^{\dagger}(U)V)-A^{\dagger}(X)A^{\dagger}(V)U))

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(X,Y)U=

\displaystyle R^{0,1}(X,Y)U+(1-t^{2})(A(X)A^{\dagger}(Y)-A(Y)A^{\dagger}(X))U

\displaystyle R_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(X,Y)U=

\displaystyle R^{1,0}(X,Y)U.

By taking the trace, we further deduce the following properties of the Ricci-curvature

\displaystyle Ric_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(X,Y)=

\displaystyle(1-t^{2})p^{*}Ric_{g_{S}}(X,Y)+t^{2}Ric^{1,0}(X,Y)

\displaystyle Ric_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(X,Y)=

\displaystyle Ric(X,Y)-\frac{1}{3}(1-t^{2})(p^{*}Ric_{g_{S}}(X,Y)-Ric^{1,0}(X,Y))

\displaystyle Ric_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(X,U)=

\displaystyle t^{2}Ric^{1,0}(X,U)

\displaystyle Ric_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(X,U)=

\displaystyle Ric^{0,1}(X,U)+(1-t^{2}){\mathrm{tr}}\{V\mapsto A(X){\mathrm{II}}(U,V)-\left<A^{\dagger}(X)U,k^{\sharp}(V)\right>\}

\displaystyle Ric_{g^{t}}^{1,0}(U,V)=

\displaystyle t^{2}\left(Ric^{1,0}(U,V)-(1-t^{2})\left<{\mathrm{tr}}_{H}(AA^{\dagger}U),V\right>\right)

\displaystyle Ric_{g^{t}}^{0,1}(U,V)=

\displaystyle(1-t^{2})Ric_{g_{V}}(U,V)+t^{2}Ric^{0,1}(U,V)

as well as the scalar curvature

\displaystyle{\mathrm{scal}}_{g^{t}}=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{tr}}_{HH}Ric_{g^{t}}+t^{-1}\cdot{\mathrm{tr}}_{HV}Ric_{g^{t}}+t^{-2}\cdot{\mathrm{tr}}_{VV}Ric_{g^{t}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathrm{tr}}_{HH}Ric_{g}+\frac{2}{3}(1-t^{2})\cdot(p^{*}{\mathrm{scal}}_{g_{S}}-{\mathrm{tr}}_{HH}Ric_{g}^{1,0})

\displaystyle+t^{-1}\cdot{\mathrm{tr}}_{HV}(Ric_{g}^{0,1}+t\cdot{\mathrm{tr}}_{HV}Ric_{g}^{1,0})

\displaystyle+t^{-1}(1-t^{2})\cdot({\mathrm{tr}}_{HV}{\mathrm{tr}}_{VV}A{\mathrm{II}}-\left<A^{\dagger},k^{\sharp}\right>)

\displaystyle+t^{-2}(1-t^{2})\cdot({\mathrm{scal}}_{g_{V}}-t^{2}\cdot{\mathrm{tr}}_{VV}{\mathrm{tr}}_{HH}AA^{\dagger})+{\mathrm{tr}}_{VV}Ric_{g}

Appendix B
The Spectrum of Dirac Operators on Spheres

Using the results of Bär
[
Bär96
]
and Ikeda and Taniguchi
[
IT78
]
we are able compute the spectra of geometric Dirac operators such as the trivially twisted Spin-Dirac operator or the Hodge-de Rham operator on (orbifold) space-forms.

The key idea in these constructions is that that (orbifold) space forms are homogenous spaces. Thus the spectral theory boils down to representation theory and the eigenvalues and their multiplicity can be combinatorially determined.

These eigenvalues and their multiplicity are needed in section
4.2.1
in order to determine the critical rates of the normal operator
\widehat{D}_{0}
and accordingly of the operators
D
and
\widehat{D}^{t}_{\zeta}
.

B.1.
The Spectrum of the Hodge-de Rham Operator

In the following we will regard the round
m-1
-sphere as the homogeneous space

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{m-1}={\mathrm{SO}}(m)/{\mathrm{SO}}(m-1).

The spectral theory of
\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
is hence related to the Kasimir-operator of
{\mathrm{SO}}(m)
. The following Lemma summarises the results in
[
IT78
]
.

Theorem B.1

(
[
IT78
]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
be equipped with the round metric. The
L^{2}
-space of complex valued forms decomposes into irreducible
{\mathrm{SO}}(m)
-representations of eigenforms. The decomposition is given by

•

(
m-1=2n
)

\displaystyle L^{2}\Omega^{q}(\mathbb{S}^{m-1},\mathbb{C})=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcll}\Phi^{0}_{k}&\oplus&\Phi^{0,1}_{k}&q=0\\
\Phi^{q,0}_{k}&\oplus&\Phi^{q,1}_{k}&1\leq q\leq n-2\\
\Phi^{q,0}_{k}&\oplus&\Psi^{q,1}_{k}&q=n-1\\
&\Psi^{q,0}_{k}&&q=n\end{array}\right.

{\mathrm{SO}}(2n+1)
-representation

eigenvalue

\Phi^{0}_{k}

k(k+n-1)

\Phi^{q,0}_{k}

(k+q)(k+n+1-q)

\Phi^{q,1}_{k}

(k+q+1)(k+n-q)

\Psi^{q,0}_{k}

(k+q)(k+q+1)

\Psi^{q,1}_{k}

(k+q+1)(k+q+2)

•

(
m-1=2n-1
)

\displaystyle L^{2}\Omega^{q}(\mathbb{S}^{m-1},\mathbb{C})=\bigoplus_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\left\{\begin{array}[]{cccccl}&\Phi^{0}_{k}&\oplus&\Phi^{0,1}_{k}&&q=0\\
&\Phi^{q,0}_{k}&\oplus&\Phi^{q,1}_{k}&&1\leq q\leq n-3\\
&\Phi^{q,0}_{k}&\oplus&\Psi^{q,1}_{k}&&q=n-2\\
\vartheta^{q,0}_{k}&\oplus&\Psi^{q,0}_{k}&\oplus&\vartheta^{q,1}_{k}&q=n-1\end{array}\right.

{\mathrm{SO}}(2n)
-representation

eigenvalue

\Phi^{0}_{k}

k(k+n-1)

\Phi^{q,0}_{k}

(k+q)(k+n+1-q)

\Phi^{q,1}_{k}

(k+q+1)(k+n-q)

\vartheta^{q,0}_{k}

(k+q)^{2}

\vartheta^{q,1}_{k}

(k+q)^{2}

\Psi^{q,0}_{k}

(k+q-1)(k+q+1)

\Psi^{q,1}_{k}

(k+q-1)(k+q+1)

.

\displaystyle\beta_{\pm}\in

where
\lambda_{q}\in{\mathrm{Spec}}(\Delta_{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}:\Omega^{q}\rightarrow\Omega^{q})
, i.e.

q

k(k+n-1)
,

(k+q-1)(k+n-q-2)

(k+n-2)(k+4)

(k+1)(k+2)

1<q\leq n-3

(k+q)(k+n-q+1)
,
(k+q+1)(k+n-q)

q=n-2

(k+n-2)(k+n+3)
,
(k+n-3)(k+n-1)

q=n-1

(k+n-1)^{2}
,
(k+n-2)(k+n-1)

B.2.
The Spectrum of the Trivially Twisted Spin-Dirac Operator

Let
S\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
denote the spinor bundle on

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{m-1}={\mathrm{Spin}}(m)/{\mathrm{Spin}}(m-1),

\overline{\nabla}^{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}
the lift of the Levi-Civita,
(F,\nabla^{F})
a trivial rank
r
-vector bundle and

\displaystyle D={\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}\circ(\overline{\nabla}^{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{F})

the Dirac operator on
E=S\mathbb{S}^{m-1}\otimes F
. Let
\gamma=\pm\frac{1}{2}
, let
\Phi\in\Gamma(\mathbb{S}^{m-1},E)
and let us denote by

\displaystyle\nabla:=\overline{\nabla}^{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}\otimes 1+1\otimes\nabla^{F}.

Then
\Phi
is called a
\gamma
-Killing spinor if

\displaystyle\nabla^{\gamma}_{X}\Phi=\nabla_{X}\Phi-\gamma{\mathrm{cl}}_{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}(X)\Phi=0

holds for all
X\in\mathfrak{X}(\mathbb{S}^{m-1})
.

Lemma B.1

(
[
Bär96
]
)
.

The bundle
E
is
\nabla^{\gamma}
-trivialisable. Moreover shifted Weitzenböck-formula

\displaystyle(D+\gamma)^{2}=(\nabla^{\gamma})^{*}\nabla^{\gamma}+(m-2)^{2}

holds.

Lemma B.2
.

Let
f_{i}
denote the eigenfunctions of
\Delta_{g_{\mathbb{S}^{m-1}}}
of eigenvalue
\lambda_{i}
. Then
\Phi^{j}_{i}=f_{i}\Phi^{j}
forms a basis of

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{m-1},E)

where
\Phi^{j}
is a basis of Killing spinors.

Corollary B.1
.

The eigenvalues of
D
are

\displaystyle\pm\lambda_{k}=\pm\left(\frac{m-1}{2}+k\right)

and are of multiplicity

\displaystyle{\mathrm{m}}(\lambda_{k})=r\cdot 2^{[\frac{m-1}{2}]}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}k+m-2\\
k\end{array}\right).

Let in the following
m
be even and
\Gamma\subset{\mathrm{SO}}(m)
a finite subgroup.

Lemma B.3
.

Spin structures on
\mathbb{S}^{m-1}/\Gamma
are in one to one correspondence with lifts

\displaystyle\epsilon:\Gamma\rightarrow{\mathrm{Spin}}(m).

and the bundle
E
descends to
E/\epsilon(\Gamma)
on
\mathbb{S}^{m-1}
.

Lemma B.4

(
[
Bär96
]
)
.

The eigenvalues of the Dirac operator on
E/\epsilon(\Gamma)
are

\displaystyle\pm\lambda_{k}=\pm\left(\frac{m-1}{2}+k\right)

an are of multiplicity

\displaystyle{\mathrm{m}}(\pm\lambda_{k})

determined by the generating functions

\displaystyle F_{\pm}(z)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{\mathrm{m}}(\pm(\frac{m-1}{2}+k),D)z^{k}=\frac{r}{|\Gamma|}\sum_{g\in\Gamma}\frac{\chi_{\mp}(\epsilon(g))-z\cdot\chi_{\pm}(\epsilon(g))}{{\mathrm{det}}(1_{m-2}-z\cdot g)}

where
\chi_{\pm}
are the characters of the irreducible half-spin representations

\displaystyle\rho_{\pm}:{\mathrm{Spin}}(m)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Aut}}(\Sigma^{\pm}).
